 \{2r\le\mathcal{A}_1(0)-(\mathcal{A}_\ell(-y)+y^2)-(\mathcal{S}(z,0)+z^2)+ \mathcal{S}(z,-y)+(z+y)^2\}\,.
\end{equation}
The random variables $\mathcal{S}(z,0)+z^2, \mathcal{S}(z,-y)+(z+y)^2$, and $\mathcal{A}_1(0)$ each have the GUE
Tracy--Widom distribution. Indeed, this follows from coupling in \ref{coupling: Airy sheet}, which gives $\mathcal{S}(0,\cdot)=\mathcal{A}_1$.
Also $\mathcal{A}_\ell(-y)+y^2\overset{d}=\mathcal{A}_\ell(0)$ by the stationarity of the
parabolic shifted line ensemble. 

A union bound and the above distributional identities give,
\begin{align*}
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(Z_\ell(x,0)\le-y\bigr)
 &\le \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_1(0)\ge r/2\bigr)
      +\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_\ell(0)\le-r/2\bigr)\\
 &\quad+\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\mathcal{S}(x,0)+x^2\le-r/2\bigr)
      +\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\mathcal{S}(z,-y)+(z+y)^2\ge r/2\bigr).
\end{align*}
The Tracy--Widom upper and lower tails in
\cite[Theorem~2.3]{DOV}, and Lemma~\ref{lemma: airy counting 1}, imply
\[
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(Z_\ell(x,0)\le-y\bigr)
 \le C \mathrm{e}^{-cr^{3/2}}+\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_\ell(0)\le-r/2\bigr)\,,
\]
for constants $c, C > 0$. Lemma \ref{lemma: airy counting 1} gives $\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_\ell(0)\le-r/2\bigr)\le C\mathrm{e}^{\ell-cr^{3/2}}$ for constants $c, C> 0$. Combining the above, we arrive at 
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(Z_\ell(x,0)\le-y\bigr) \le C\cdot \exp (\ell - ca^{\frac{3}{2}})\,,
\]
for constants $c, C> 0$.

To estimate the first disjointness probability, we simply use Theorem \ref{thm: semi-inf tail disj proba}. Combining the above with $z = x+\varepsilon$ (noting $\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$) gives
\[
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\mathsf{LV}\big(\Delta^{(0,1)}_{(0,\ell)}(u)\,, u\in (x-\varepsilon, x+\varepsilon)\big)\neq\emptyset\bigg)\le C\cdot y \cdot \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot \mathrm{e}^{|\log\varepsilon|^{\frac{5}{6}}} + C\cdot \exp\left(\ell-c y ^\frac{3}{2}\right)\,.
\]
Taking $y = O((\log(1/\varepsilon))^{\frac{2}{3}})$ gives the result.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop: local variation geod disj}
    Almost surely, for all $\ell \ge 1$,
    \begin{align*}
       \bigg\{x>0:  \pi^L[x\to (0, 1)]\text{ and } \pi^R[x\to (0, \ell)] \text{ are disjoint} \bigg\} = \mathsf{LV}((0, \infty), \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0,  \ell)})\,.
    \end{align*}
    In particular, this set of geodesic disjointness is closed and nowhere dense.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
We proceed by establishing the two inclusions separately.

\underline{$\subseteq$}: Suppose $x>0$ is such that $\pi^L[x\to (0, 1)]$ an $\pi^R[x\to (0, 1)]$ are disjoint (recall Definition \ref{def: disjointness}). Then by monotonicity of geodesics, item $(1)$ of Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, for all rational $q< x< r$, we have the \emph{unique} (by item $(2)$ of Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}) geodesics $\pi[q\to (0, 1)]$ an $\pi[r\to (0, 1)]$ are disjoint.

Thus, we have arguing in exactly the same way as in the proof of \ref{prop: non-disj-close-endpts-inf-geo},
\[
\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0,  \ell)}(r) > \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0,  \ell)}(q)\,.
\]
Since $q, r\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}$ were arbitrary, we conclude that $x\in \mathsf{LV}(\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}, (0, \infty))$, concluding the proof of the first part.

\underline{$\supseteq$}: For this part suppose $x\in \mathsf{LV}(\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}, (0, \infty))$. We proceed by contradiction. 

If $\pi^L[x\to (0, 1)]$ an $\pi^R[x\to (0, \ell)]$ are \emph{not} disjoint, then their zigzags (recall \eqref{eq: zig-zag}) must share some common point $(z, m)$ with $m\ge \ell$, $z \le 0$. By approximation, Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx}, for any fixed $t < 0$, there exist $q< x< r$ rational such that the restrictions of the zig-zags of $\pi[q\to (0, 1)]$ and $\pi^L[x\to (0, 1)]$ to $[t, 0]$ coincide and similarly for $\pi[r\to (0, \ell)]$ and $\pi^R[x\to (0, \ell)]$. By monotonicity this is true for all $q< q'< x< r'< r$ replacing $q$ with $q'$ and likewise $r$ with $r'$. Taking $t< z$ we see that $\pi[q\to (0, 1)]$ and $\pi[r\to (0, \ell)]$ share the point $(z, m)$. Now arguing as in the previous part and using Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing equiavlence}, instead of a strict inequality the non-disjointness forces an equality, whence we conclude 
\[
\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0,  \ell)}(r) = \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0,  \ell)}(q)\,.
\]
By monotonicity, this forces $\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0,  \ell)}$ to be constant on the interval $(q, r)$, a contradiction to $x\in \mathsf{LV}(\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}, (0, \infty))$ concluding the proof.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lemma: asymptotic non degeneracy ell}
   Fix $\ell \ge 2$. Then for any fixed $\varepsilon > 0$ sufficiently small, 
   \[
     \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \bigg(\pi[x-\varepsilon \to (0, 1)] \;\;\; \mathrm{and} \;\;\; \pi[x+\varepsilon \to (0, \ell)] \text{ are disjoint} \bigg) \to 1\,, \text{ as } \ell\to \infty\,.
    \]

    This means by Proposition \ref{prop: non-disj-close-endpts-inf-geo}, for any fixed $0< \varepsilon < x$, almost surely eventually in $\ell$, the sets of local variation $\mathsf{LV}\big(\Delta^{(0,1)}_{(0,\ell)}(u)\,, u\in (x-\varepsilon, x+\varepsilon)\big)$ are non-empty.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
By the stationarity of semi-infinite geodesics with respect to horizontal shifts, item $(3)$ of Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, for any fixed $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, the same results hold verbatim for the Busemann profile $\Delta^{(y,1)}_{(y,\ell)}$.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Fix $0< \varepsilon < x$. Observe there is some random rational $T^{\varepsilon}<0$ such that the geodesics $\pi[x-\varepsilon \to (0, 1)],\pi[x+\varepsilon \to (0, 1)]$ are disjoint on $(-\infty, T^{\varepsilon}]$. This is due to their distinct asymptotic parabolic shapes, \eqref{def: semi-inf geo}. Then, for all $\ell \ge \lim_{t\nearrow T^\varepsilon}\pi[x+\varepsilon \to (0, 1)](t)+1$, by geodesic ordering, the zig-zag of the geodesic $\pi[x+\varepsilon \to (0, \ell)]$ is disjoint from $\pi[x+\varepsilon \to (0, 1)]$. Thus, by bounded convergence, we obtain the convergence statement for the disjointness probabilities. By Proposition \ref{prop: non-disj-close-endpts-inf-geo}, we have for all $\ell$ sufficiently large, the sets of local variation $\mathsf{LV}\big(\Delta^{(0,1)}_{(0,\ell)}(u)\,, u\in (x-\varepsilon, x+\varepsilon)\big)$ are non-empty, concluding the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop: semi-inf geod jump}
    Fix $x> 0, y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ and $\ell \ge 2$. Then, with $Z_\ell(x, y)$ the jump time of the semi-infinite geodesic $\pi[x\to (y, 1)]$ from level $\ell$ to $\ell-1$, we have
    \[
    Z_\ell(x, y) \in  \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell)}^{(0, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\,,\quad \ell \ge 2.
    \]
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Fix $-\infty< u < y$, $\ell\ge 2$ and consider the almost surely unique (by the remark after Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp}) maximiser
    \[
    Z^{*}_{\ell, u}(x,y):=\displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in [u, y]}\bigg(\mathcal{A}[x_k\to (s,\ell)]-\mathcal{A}[x_k\to (0,1)]+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\,,
    \]
    where we will use the notation $x_k := -\sqrt{k}/\sqrt{2x}$, $x> 0$, $k\ge 1$. It is precisely the jump time from level $\ell$ to level $\ell-1$ of the geodesic $\pi[x_k \to (y, \ell)]$ on the event that jump time is at least $u$. Now, by Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, the semi-infinite geodesic $\pi[x\to (y, \ell)]$ can be written as the limit of the geodesics $\pi[x_k \to (y, \ell)]$ in the topology of jump times. Moreover, the convergence of jump times and the almost sure uniqueness of finite horizon geodesics in the Airy line ensemble give that for any fixed possibly random $t \le y$, the geodesics $\pi[x_k\to (y, \ell)]$ and $\pi[x\to (y, \ell)]$ eventually coincide on $[t, y]$, for all $k$ sufficiently large (see Figure \ref{fig: geod approx}). This means for all $u \le Z_\ell(x, y)$ rational, we have almost surely eventually in $k \ge 1$,
    \[
    Z_\ell(x, y) \in \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in [u, y]}\bigg(\mathcal{A}[x_k\to (s,\ell)]-\mathcal{A}[x_k\to (0,1)]+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\,,\quad \ell \ge 2.
    \]
    Equivalently, 
    \begin{align*}
         &\mathcal{A}[x_k\to (Z_\ell(x, y),\ell)]-\mathcal{A}[x_k\to (0,1)]+\mathcal{A}[(Z_\ell(x, y), \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\\
         &\ge \mathcal{A}[x_k\to (s,\ell)]-\mathcal{A}[x_k\to (0,1)]+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\,,\quad s\in [u, y]\,.
    \end{align*}
    Taking $k\to \infty$, using the Busemann limit definition \eqref{eq: Airy limit busemann} and then sending $u\to - \infty$, concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop: semi-inf geod jump simultaneous}
    Almost surely, for all $x> 0, y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ and $\ell \ge 2$, $\square \in \{L, R\}$, the semi-infinite geodesic jump times $Z^\square_\ell(x, y)$ of the geodesics $\pi^\square [x\to (y, \ell)]$ from level $\ell$ to $\ell-1$ satisfy
    \[
    Z^\square_\ell(x, y) \in  \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta^{\square, (0,1)}_{(s, \ell-1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]-\mathcal{A}_1(y)\bigg)\,,\quad \ell \ge 2.
    \]
    Moreover, almost surely for all $x> 0$, $A_x := \{y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}: Z^L_2(x, y) = Z^R_2(x, y)\}$ has Hausdorff dimension at least one half and is unbounded.
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump}, we have almost surely for all rational $q > 0, r$ the jump times satisfy
    \[
    Z_\ell(q, r) \in \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, r]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{(0, 1)}(q)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (r,1)]\bigg)\,.
    \]
    In other words, for all $s\le r$,
    \[
    \Delta_{(Z_\ell(q, r) , \ell-1)}^{(0, 1)}(q)+\mathcal{A}[(Z_\ell(q, r) , \ell-1)\to (r,1)]\ge \Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{(0, 1)}(q)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (r,1)]\,.
    \]
    Now, we have using the approximation lemma, Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx} that almost surely simultaneously for all $x > 0, y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, the convergence of jump times $Z^\square_\ell(x, y) = \lim_n Z_\ell(x_n, y_n)$ where $x_n>0, y_n$ are rational and increase/decrease respectively to $x, y$, depending on whether $\square = L, R$. 

    Thus, for fixed $s\le y$, we have using the limit definition \ref{eq: busemann cadlag/caglad} and the regularity of the Busemann processes, Proposition \ref{prop: Busemann regularity} for all $s\le y$,
    \[
    \Delta_{(Z^\square_\ell(x, y) , \ell-1)}^{\square, (0, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(Z^\square_\ell(x, y) , \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\ge \Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{\square, (0, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\,.
    \]
    
    To bound the Hausdorff dimensions of $A_x$ from below simultaneously for all $x> 0$, observe that with the coupling between the Airy line ensemble and the Airy sheet \ref{coupling: Airy sheet}, we have
    \[
     F^\square_\ell(x, y): y\mapsto \displaystyle\operatorname*{max}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{\square, (0, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]-\mathcal{A}_1(y)\bigg) = \mathcal{S}(x, y)-\mathcal{S}(x, 0)-\mathcal{S}(0, y)\,.
    \]
    In particular, this follows from an application of the approximation lemma, Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx} and the continuity of the Airy sheet $\mathcal{S}$, using the fact that this equality is achieved for all rational $x, y$. We thus have almost surely for all $x> 0 , y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$
    \begin{align*}
        &\Delta_{(Z^L_\ell(x, y), \ell-1)}^{L, (0, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(Z^L_\ell(x, y), \ell-1)\to (y,1)]-\mathcal{A}_1(y)\\
        = &\Delta_{(Z^R_\ell(x, y), \ell-1)}^{R, (0, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(Z^R_\ell(x, y), \ell-1)\to (y,1)]-\mathcal{A}_1(y)\\
        = &\mathcal{S}(x, y)-\mathcal{S}(x, 0)-\mathcal{S}(0, y)\,.
    \end{align*}

    Observe that by the quadrangle inequality for the Airy sheet \eqref{eq: quadrangle ineq airy sheet}, $F^\square(x, \cdot)$ is almost surely non-decreasing almost surely for all $x > 0$. Now, by the global shape estimates for the Airy sheet, \eqref{eq: airy shape bnds} we have almost surely, for all $x> 0$, $F^\square_\ell(x, y)\to \infty$ as $y\to \infty$. We now show that almost surely, simultaneously for all $x > 0$, the set of local variation of $F^\square_\ell(x, \cdot)$ has Hausdorff dimension at least $1/2$ and is unbounded. 

    Indeed, we proceed by a deterministic argument on the event of full probability of the H\"{o}lder $1/2^{-}$-continuity of the Airy sheet and the aforementioned divergence of $F^\square_\ell(x, \cdot)$ simultaneously for all $x > 0$. For $0< a< b$, suppose $\mathsf{LV}(\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot), [a, b])$ is non-empty and has Hausdorff dimension $\delta < 1/2$. By the sub-additivity of Hausdorff measures, there exists some interval $[a, b]\subset \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ so that $\mathsf{LV}(\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot), [a, b])$ has Hausdorff dimension $\delta$. This means that for all $\varepsilon > 0$, there exists some cover of $\mathsf{LV}(\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot), [a, b])$ by $N_\varepsilon$ many intervals $(I^\varepsilon_i)_i$ of diameter $\varepsilon$ such that
    \[
    \sum^{N_\varepsilon}_{i=1} \operatorname{diam}(I^\varepsilon_i)^{\delta}\to 0\,,\qquad \varepsilon \to 0\,.
    \]
    We thus estimate by the monotonicity of $\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot)$, which follows from the quadrangle identity in \eqref{eq: quadrangle ineq airy sheet}, and the H\"{o}lder $1/2^-$-continuity of $\mathcal{S}$ (Section \ref{sec: preliminaries}),
    \begin{align*}
        0 &< \mathcal{S}(x, b) - \mathcal{S}(0, b) -  \mathcal{S}(x, a) - \mathcal{S}(0, a)\\
        &\le \sum^{N_\varepsilon}_{i=1}\mathcal{S}(x, \sup I^\varepsilon_i) - \mathcal{S}(0, \sup I^\varepsilon_i) -  \mathcal{S}(x, \inf I^\varepsilon_i) - \mathcal{S}(0, \inf I^\varepsilon_i)\\
        &\le C_\delta\cdot \sum^{N_\varepsilon}_{i=1} \operatorname{diam}(I^\varepsilon_i)^{\delta + \frac{1/2 - \delta}{2}}\le C_\delta\cdot \varepsilon^{\frac{1/2 - \delta}{2}}\cdot \sum^{N_\varepsilon}_{i=1} \operatorname{diam}(I^\varepsilon_i)^{\delta}\,,
    \end{align*}
    for some constant $C_\delta > 0$. By assumption the right hand side converges to zero as $\varepsilon \to 0$, a contradiction to the non-constancy of $\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot)$ on $[a, b]$. To establish the lower bound on the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathsf{LV}(\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot), (0, \infty))$ and show it is unbounded, repeat this argument on intervals $[a, b]$ of non-constancy of $F^\square_\ell(x, \cdot)$ for arbitrarily large $a, b$, guaranteed to exist by the above divergence of the values of $F^\square_\ell(x, y)$ as $y\to \infty$.
    
    For $\ell = 2$, we obtain using the coupling \ref{coupling: Airy sheet},
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\mathcal{S}(x, s)-\mathcal{S}(0, s)\bigg)\,, \qquad x \ge 0, y \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\,.
    \]
    Note due to the asymptotic parabolic shape of the Airy sheet, \eqref{eq: airy shape bnds} the expression above is well defined for all $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}, x> 0$. As before, the set of local variation of 
    \[
    y\mapsto \max_{s\in (-\infty, y]}(\mathcal{S}(x, s)-\mathcal{S}(0, s)) = \mathcal{S}(x, y) -\mathcal{S}(0, y)
    \]
    has Hausdorff dimension at least one half and is unbounded. Now there is an at most countable set $N$ of boundary points of intervals of constancy of the above Airy difference profile. Thus, on an event of full probability, on $\mathsf{LV}(\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot), (0, \infty))\setminus N$, which has Hausdorff dimension a least one half, for all $y\in K$, $\mathcal{S}(x, y) -\mathcal{S}(0, y) > \mathcal{S}(x, u) -\mathcal{S}(0, u)$ for all $u < y$, and so $Z^L_2(x, y) = Z^R_2(x, y)$. We thus obtain the inclusion $\mathsf{LV}(\mathcal{S}(x, \cdot) - \mathcal{S}(0, \cdot), (0, \infty))\setminus N \subseteq A_x$, concluding the proof.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop: non-degen asymptotic}
    For all $\ell\ge 2$,
    \begin{equation*}
	\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\mathsf{LV}\big(\Delta^{(0,1)}_{(0,\ell)}(u)\,, u\in (0, \infty)\big)\neq\emptyset\bigg)=1\,.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
First observe that almost surely, for all $x>0$ we have $Z^\square_2(x, 0)< 0$. By monotonicity of jump times it suffices to consider $x>0$ rational. To see this, by the asymptotic parabolic shape of $\pi[x\to (0,1)]$ there exists $q< 0$ rational and $k\ge 2$ such that $(q, k)$ lies on a horizontal segment of the zigzag (recall from \eqref{eq: zig-zag}) of $\pi[x\to (0,1)]$. Now, by the restriction property of semi-infinite geodesics, we have the path $\pi[x\to (0, 1)]|_{[q, 0]}$ is the unique (by the remark after Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp}) geodesic in the Airy line ensemble from $(q, k)$ to $(0, 1)$. Since the latter has an almost surely positive first jump time by Lemma 6.1 in \cite{tassopoulos2026kpzfixedpointbrownian}), the claim follows.
    
    \noindent \underline{Claim}:
    Fix $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ and $\ell\ge 2$. As $x\to \infty$, we have almost surely that the geodesic jump times $Z^\square_\ell(x, y)\to 0$.

    By monotonicity of jump times, it suffices to consider $x> 0$ rational. By Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump}, we have that 
     \[
    Z_\ell(x, y) \in  \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell)}^{(y, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\,,\quad \ell \ge 2.
    \]
    Now for any fixed $\ell\ge 2$, $a< y$, we have by the metric composition law, Lemma \ref{lemma: Metric Composition}, 
    \[
    \max_{s\in (-\infty, a]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell)}^{(y, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\le \mathcal{S}(x, a)-\mathcal{S}(x,y)+ \mathcal{A}[(a, \ell)\to (y, 1)]\,,
    \]
    while evaluating at the right boundary, we have (for $x> 1$)
    \[
     \max_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell)}^{(y, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(s, \ell-1)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\ge \Delta_{(y, \ell)}^{(y, 1)}(x)\ge \Delta_{(y, \ell)}^{(y, 1)}(1) > -\infty
    \]
    where the last inequality comes from monotonicity of Busemann functions, Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing}. Thus, taking $x\to \infty$, using the stationarity of $\mathcal{S}(x, y) +(x-y)^2$, $x, y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ and the global bounds of the Airy sheet \eqref{eq: airy shape bnds}, we see as $x\to \infty$, $\mathcal{S}(x, a)-\mathcal{S}(x,y)\to -\infty$ and so eventually in $x > 0$, $Z_\ell(x, y) > a$. Taking $a\nearrow y$ concludes the proof of the claim.
    
    For any $x> 0$, taking any rational $x'> x$, by the asymptotic parabolic shape of semi-infinite geodesics, we have there exists some $k\ge 1$ such that for all $k'\ge k$, the jump times satisfy $Z_{k'}(x, 0) < Z_{k'}(x', 0)$. By the above claim, we can take some rational $x''> x'$ sufficiently large so that $Z_{k}(x'',0)>Z_2(x',0)$. Thus, the geodesics $\pi[x\to 0]$ and $\pi[x''\to (0, \ell)]$ are disjoint (as zigzags). Finally, by an application of Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing} we have that $\Delta^{(0,1)}_{(0,\ell)}(x'')>\Delta^{(0,1)}_{(0,\ell)}(x)$, completing the proof.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm: haus dim ell}
    For all $\ell \ge 2$ and $\square \in \{L, R\}$, we have almost surely,
    \[
    0 \le \mathrm{dim}_\mathscr{H} (\mathsf{LV}(\Delta^{\square, (0,1)}_{(0, \ell)}, (0, \infty))) \le \frac{1}{2}\,.
    \]
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{prop: non-disj-close-endpts-inf-geo}, we have for all rational $0 < \varepsilon < x$ the estimates
    \begin{equation*}
	\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\mathsf{LV}\big(\Delta_{(0, \ell)}^{(0, 1)}\,, [x-\varepsilon, x+\varepsilon]\big)\neq\emptyset\bigg)\le C_x \cdot \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)}
	\end{equation*}
    for some constant $C_x > 0$ that is uniformly bounded when $x$ is on a bounded subset of $(0, \infty)$, bounded away from zero. 
    
    Now, for any $a < b$ finite, consider the interval $[a, b]$. For any $\varepsilon > 0$, starting from $a$, partition $[a, b]$ into a family of intervals of diameter $2\varepsilon$, possibly overshooting $b$ in the last step. Let $N_\varepsilon\le \lceil(b-a)/(2\varepsilon)\rceil$ be the random number of intervals above for which $\Delta_{(0, \ell)}^{(0, 1)}$ experiences an increase. Using linearity and taking expectations, we have using Markov's inequality the estimate
    \[
    \mathbb{E}[N_\varepsilon] \le \bigg\lceil\frac{b-a}{2\varepsilon}\bigg\rceil\cdot\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \bigg(\mathsf{LV}(\Delta_{(0, \ell)}^{(0, 1)}, [x-\varepsilon, x+\varepsilon])\neq \emptyset \bigg)\le C_{a, b}\cdot\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)}
    \]
    for some constant $C_{a, b} > 0$.

    We can thus cover the set of local variation $\mathsf{LV}(\Delta_{(0, \ell)}^{(0, 1)}, [a, b])$ by $N_\varepsilon$ many intervals $(U^i_\varepsilon)_i$ of diameter $2\varepsilon$ such that for any fixed $\delta > 0$,
    \[
    \sum_i \operatorname{diam}(U^i_\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}\le C_\delta  \cdot N_\varepsilon\cdot \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}\,.
    \]
    for some constant $C_\delta > 0$. Taking expectations, we have
    \[
     \mathbb{E}[\sum_i \operatorname{diam}(U^i_\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}]\le C_{a, b, \delta}\cdot \varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)} \cdot \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta} \le C_{a, b, \delta} \cdot \varepsilon^{\delta+ o_\varepsilon(1)}\,, 
    \]
    which converges to zero as $\varepsilon \to 0$. Thus, almost surely, the Hausdorff dimension of $\Delta_{(0, \ell)}^{(0, 1)}$ is at most $1/2 +\delta$. Since $\delta$, $a< b$ were arbitrary, we obtain that the Hausdorff dimension of  $\mathsf{LV}(\Delta_{(0, \ell)}^{(0, 1)}, (0, \infty))$ is at most $1/2$, concluding the proof of the upper bound.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop: gap 2 cont}
    We in fact show that almost surely for all $s\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ the Busemann profiles $(0, \infty) \ni x\mapsto \Delta^{\square, (s,1)}_{(s, 2)}(x)$ on $(0, \infty)$ are H\"{o}lder $1/2^-$-continuous on $(0, \infty)$.

    This means almost surely for all $s\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ the Busemann profiles $\Delta^{L, (s,1)}_{(s, 2)}$ and $\Delta^{R, (s,1)}_{(s, 2)}$ almost surely agree on $(0, \infty)$.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
The following argument is deterministic on the event of semi-infinite  geodesic existence and uniqueness (see Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}). In the remainder, we set $s= 0$. 

      In the argument that follows, we will be considering general $\ell\ge 2$ and only use this fact in the end. Fix any $x_0 > 0$. By shift invariance of geodesics, item $(4)$ of Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, there exists some random $Y_0\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$ such that for all $x> x_0$, geodesics $\pi^\square[x\to (Y_0, 1)]$ satisfy $\pi^\square[x\to (Y_0, 1)](0)\ge \ell$ and so by monotonicity of geodesics, have for all $x> x_0$, $\pi^\square[x\to (Y_0, 1)]\ge \pi^\square[x\to (0, \ell)]$ on $(-\infty, 0]$. Moreover, by Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump simultaneous} we can deterministically take $Y\ge Y_0$ (possibly depending on $x$) such that $Z^L_\ell(x, Y) = Z^R_\ell(x, Y)$, when $\ell = 2$ (we will later see this will always be the case for general $\ell\ge 2$).

     Now, take any $x>x_0$ and sequences of rational numbers $x_n < x <  y_n$ with $\inf_n x_n > x_0$ and $x_n - y_n \to 0$ as $n\to \infty$. The proof of Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump} gives that almost surely for all $x\in  (0,\infty)\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}} , y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}$ we have for the geodesic jump times $Z_k(x, y)$, $k\ge \ell$ (recall notation from Section \ref{sec: semi-inf geod})
     \begin{align*}
         &\lim_{m\to \infty}(\mathcal{A}[x_m\to (Z_k(x, y), k-1)]-\mathcal{A}[x_m\to (0, \ell-1)])+\mathcal{A}[x\to (Z_k(x, y), k-1)]\to (y, \ell-1)]\\
         = &\lim_{m\to \infty}(\mathcal{A}[x_m\to (y, \ell-1)]-\mathcal{A}[x_m\to (0, \ell-1)])\,.
     \end{align*}
     In terms of Busemann processes this reads for $y>0$ rational, 
     \[
     \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(Z_k(x, y), k-1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[x\to (Z_k(x, y), k-1)] = \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(y, \ell-1)}(x)\,.
     \]
     Thus, by the approximation Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx}, we have for any $u> 0 , v\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ (noting the convergence of jump times and regularity of Busemann processes, Proposition \ref{prop: Busemann regularity}), 
      \[
     \Delta^{\square, (0, 1)}_{(Z^\square_k(u, v), k-1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}[(Z^\square_k(u, v), k-1)] = \Delta^{\square, (0, 1)}_{(v, \ell-1)}(x)\,.
     \]
     Thus, with $u = x_n, y_n, v = Y$, using that $Z^L_\ell(x, Y) = Z^R_\ell(x, Y)$ and for $\ell = 2$ $\Delta^{L, (0, 1)}_{(Y, \ell-1)} = \Delta^{R, (0, 1)}_{(Y, \ell-1)}$ $= \mathcal{S}(\cdot, Y)-\mathcal{S}(\cdot, 0)$, we have with $k = \ell+1$ (by Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx} for all $n$ sufficiently large the jump times $Z^\square_\ell(x_n, Y) = Z^\square_\ell(y_n, Y) = Z_\ell(x, Y)$)    
    \[
     \Delta^{\square, (0, 1)}_{(Z_\ell(x, Y), \ell)}(y_n)-\Delta^{\square, (0, 1)}_{(Z_\ell(x, Y), \ell)}(x_n) = \mathcal{S}(y_n, Y)-\mathcal{S}(y_n, 0)-(\mathcal{S}(x_n, Y)-\mathcal{S}(x_n, 0))\,.
     \]
     For the displayed equality we used again that $\ell = 2$.
     
     Then, we estimate using Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing} and that $Z_\ell(x, Y)$ can be taken to be strictly greater than zero
     \[
     0\le \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(y_n)-\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(x_n)\le \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(q, \ell)}(y_n)-\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(q, \ell)}(x_n)\to 0 
     \]
     for all $0< q< Z_\ell(x, y)$ and so by continuity in the second variable (Proposition \ref{prop: Busemann regularity}), we have
     \[
     0\le \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(y_n)-\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(x_n)\le \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(Z_\ell(x, Y), \ell)}(y_n)-\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(Z_\ell(x, Y), \ell)}(x_n) = \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(Y, \ell-1)}(y_n)-\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(Y, \ell-1)}(x_n)\to 0
     \]
     as $n\to \infty$.
     
    Since $x_0> 0$ was arbitrary, we conclude the proof for $s = 0$. To obtain the result almost surely simultaneously for all other $s\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, observe that by Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing} for all $0<x< y$ rational (two applications of the crossing lemma, Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing}, see also Proposition \ref{prop: Busemann regularity})
\[
\Delta^{\square, (s,1)}_{(s, 2)}(y)-\Delta^{\square, (s,1)}_{(s, 2)}(x) \le  \Delta^{\square, (\lfloor s\rfloor,1)}_{(\lceil s\rceil, 2)}(y)-\Delta^{\square, (\lfloor s\rfloor,1)}_{(\lceil s\rceil, 2)}(x)\,.
\]
Then the remaining argument with $\lfloor s\rfloor$, $\lceil s \rceil$ integers is essentially identical to that with setting $s = 0$ (see Figure \ref{fig: cont busemann}).
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm: busemann cont}
     Almost surely, for all $\ell \ge 2, s\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, the Busemann processes $\Delta^{(s,1)}_{(s, \ell)}(x)$ on $(0, \infty)$ are H\"{o}lder $1/2^-$-continuous on $(0, \infty)$.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
The proof proceeds by induction. The base step $\ell=2$ was proved in Proposition \ref{prop: gap 2 cont}. To prove the inductive step for some $\ell$, repeat the argument in Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump simultaneous} using the fact that by the induction hypothesis, $(x, s)\mapsto \Delta^{\square, (0, 1)}_{(s, \ell-1)}(x)$ are jointly continuous and so by planarity, Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing} (see also Proposition \ref{prop: Busemann regularity}) $\Delta^{\square, (0, \ell)}_{(s, \ell-1)}$ are identical on $(0, \infty)$ for all $s\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. This follows since we can write $\Delta^{\square, (0, 1)}_{(s, \ell-1)} = \Delta^{\square, (s,1)}_{(s, \ell-1)}+ \Delta^{\square, (0, 1)}_{(s, 1)}$, the former by induction and the latter continuous using the coupling in \ref{coupling: Airy sheet}.
    Then argue as in Propositions \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump} and \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump simultaneous} to show almost surely for all $x > 0, y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, the jump times $Z^\square_\ell(x, y)$ of $\pi^\square[x\to (y, \ell-1)]$ from level $\ell$ to level $\ell-1$ satisfy
    \[
    Z^\square_\ell(x, y) \in  \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{(0, \ell)}(x)-\mathcal{A}_{\ell-1}(s)\bigg)\,.
    \]
    The shift invariance of geodesics, item $(4)$ of Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties} gives that almost surely for all $x> 0 , y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ $Z^\square_\ell(x, y) \to \infty$ as $y\to \infty$. This means as $y\to \infty$, 
    \[
     \displaystyle\operatorname*{infargmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{(0, \ell)}(x)-\mathcal{A}_{\ell-1}(s)\bigg)\to \infty
    \]
    as $y\to \infty$. This means
    \[
     y\mapsto \displaystyle\operatorname*{max}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{(0, \ell)}(x)-\mathcal{A}_{\ell-1}(s)\bigg) = \Delta_{(y, \ell-1)}^{(0, \ell)}(x)-\mathcal{A}_{\ell-1}(y)
    \]
    cannot be eventually constant (the last equality follows from the metric composition law, Lemma \ref{lemma: Metric Composition}). Since then
    \[
     \sup_{y> 0}\displaystyle\operatorname*{infargmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, \ell-1)}^{(0, \ell)}(x)-\mathcal{A}_{\ell-1}(s)\bigg)< \infty\,.
    \]
    a contradiction.

    Now, arguing as in the last part of the proof of Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump simultaneous}, we obtain that almost surely, for all $x> 0$ the sets $A_x = \{Z^L_\ell(x, y) = Z^R_\ell(x,y)\}$ has Hausdorff dimension at least $1/2$. We can thus finally proceed as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop: gap 2 cont} (essentially as in Figure \ref{fig: cont busemann} replacing the depths $1, 2$ with $\ell-1, \ell$ respectively, and the Airy sheet with the Busemann functions $\Delta^{\square, (0, \ell)}_{ (s, \ell-1)}$, assumed continuous by the induction hypothesis), which concludes the proof of the inductive step.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm: Busemann equality in law}
    Fix $\ell\ge 1$. Then there exists a coupling of an Airy line ensemble $\mathcal{A}$ and a standard Airy sheet $\mathcal{S}$ where the Busemann functions $ \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(x)$ can be characterised as follows on paths $\mathcal{C}(0, \infty)$,
    \begin{align*}
        \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(x) = U_\ell(x) - \mathcal{S}(x, 0)+ \mathcal{A}_\ell(0) \,, \quad x > 0\,,
    \end{align*}
    where $U_\ell$ is a continuous stochastic process equal in law on $\mathcal{C}(0, \infty)$ to $\mathcal{A}[(0, \ell) \to (x, 1)]$.
    Moreover, for any fixed $z\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ and $0< u< v$, we have the following characterisation of the increments of the Busemann functions $\Delta^{(z, 1)}_{(z, \ell)}$,
    \[
    \Delta^{(z, 1)}_{(z, \ell)}(v)-\Delta^{(z, 1)}_{(z, \ell)}(u) = U^{u, v}_\ell -\mathcal{S}(v, z)+\mathcal{S}(u, z)+2z(v-u)\,,
    \]
    with 
    \[
    U^{u, v}_\ell\overset d = \mathcal{A}[(0, \ell) \to (v, 1)] - \mathcal{A}[(0, \ell) \to (u, 1)]\,.
    \]
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
We work with the coupling of \pageref{Skorokhod coupling} prelimiting Brownian melons $WB^n$, Airy sheets $\mathcal{S}^n$ and copies of the Airy line ensemble $\mathcal{A}$ and the Airy sheet $\mathcal{S}$. For completeness, we restate the items in the coupling below:
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] The rescaled melons $A^n$ converge to the Airy line ensemble $\mathcal{A}$ uniformly on compact subsets of $\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$.
\item [2.] $\mathcal{S}^n$ converges locally uniformly to the Airy sheet $\mathcal{S}$.
\item[3.]We have for all $(x,y)\in (\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap (0, \infty))\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}$,
\[
Z_k^n(x,y)\to Z_k(x,y) \qquad \mbox{ for all } k\ge 2\,.
\]
Moreover, as $k\to \infty$,
\begin{equation*}
Z_k(x,y)/\sqrt{k} \to -1/\sqrt{2x}.
\end{equation*}
\item[4.] For every rational triple $(x, y , z)\in (\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap (0, \infty)) \times \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}^2$ with $y < z$, there exist random rational points $0< X_1 < x < X_2$ so that the melon geodesics $\pi\{\overline{X_1}, \hat{y}\}_n$ and  $\pi\{\overline{X_2}, \hat{z}\}_n$ are not disjoint for all $n$ large enough.
\end{itemize}

Fix $n, m\ge 0$, $\ell \le n$ and a sequence $x_1, \ldots , x_m \in (0, \infty)$.
Consider the random variables
\[
\begin{aligned}
D_{n,\ell}(x_i)
&:= n^{1/6}\Bigl(
 WB^n[(2x_i n^{-1/3},n)\to(1,\ell)]
 -WB^n[(2x_i n^{-1/3},n)\to(1,1)]
 \Bigr)\,,\\
L_{n, k} &:= n^{1/6}(WB^n_k(1)-2\sqrt n) = A^n_k(0)\,,\quad 1\le k \le \ell\,,\\
\varepsilon_n(x_i) &:= \mathcal{S}^n(x, 0) - \mathcal{S}^n(0, 0) \,,\\
U_\ell(x_i)&:= \mathcal A[(0,\ell)\to(x_i,1)]\,.
\end{aligned}
\]
We aim to establish the following convergences in law,
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[
  column sep=11em,
  row sep=8em,
  every arrow/.append style={shorten <=4pt, shorten >=4pt}
]
\displaystyle
  \bigl(D_{n,\ell}(x_i)-L_{n, \ell}+\varepsilon_n(x_i)\bigr)_{i=1}^{m}
  \arrow[r, " d",
    "{\substack{\text{\cite[Lemma~6.2]{DOV}}\\ \text{ and Theorem \ref{thm: melon scaling Airy}}}}"']
  \arrow[d, " d"',
    "\text{Busemann limit}"]
&
\displaystyle
  \bigl(U_\ell(x_i)\bigr)_{i=1}^{m}
\\
\displaystyle
  \bigl(\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(x_i)+\mathcal{S}(x_i, 0)-\mathcal{A}_\ell(0)\bigr)_{i=1}^{m}
  \arrow[ur, phantom,
    "\overset{ d}{=}" description, sloped]
&
\end{tikzcd}
\]
By the pathwise construction of melons, see (17) in \cite{DOV} and their preservation of last passage values, Proposition 4.1 in \cite{DOV}, we have $A^n_1(1) = \mathcal{S}^n(0, 0)$. By \cite[Lemma 6.2]{DOV}, one can simultaneously express for all $1\le i \le m$,
\[
D_{n,\ell}(x_i)-L_{n, \ell}+\varepsilon_n(x_i) =  2x_in^{1/3}-n^{1/6}W^*_1B^n[(1-2x_i n^{-1/3},1)\to_\mathfrak{f}(1,\ell)]\,,
\]
and $W^*_1B^n$ denotes the \emph{reversed melon opened up at 1} (see equation ($26$) in \cite{DOV} and $[\cdot \to_{\mathfrak{f}}\cdot]$ denotes a \emph{backwards first} passage value (see equation ($27$) in \cite{DOV}). Importantly, by the time reversal symmetry of Brownian motion, $W^*_1B^n$ has the same law as the Brownian melon $WB^n$. The joint convergence of the rescaled backwards melons (up to the edge scaling terms) to $U_\ell(x_i)$ as $n\to \infty$ is thus established by an application of Theorem \ref{thm: melon scaling Airy} to the melons $W^*_1B^n$, noting that the backwards first passage values (which are defined analogously to the usual last passage values) are continuous functionals of their environment with respect to the topology of local uniform convergence, see (35), (36) in \cite{DOV}.

Combining the above with Theorem \ref{thm: melon scaling Airy}, we have the convergence in distribution
\[
D_{n,\ell}(x_i)-L_{n, \ell}+\varepsilon_n(x_i) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{A}[(0, \ell) \to (x, 1)]\,,\qquad n \to \infty\,.
\]

We now identify limits for individual terms on the left hand side of the above expression. Moreover, by the above coupling, $\varepsilon_n(x)$ converges to $\mathcal{A}(0)-\mathcal{S}(x, 0)$ in the topology of local uniform convergence on $\mathcal{C}$. By item 1. of the same coupling, the terms $L_{n, \ell}$ converge almost surely uniformly to $\mathcal{A}_\ell(0)$.

We now show that jointly in $1\le i \le m$, $D_{n, \ell}$ converges to $\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}(x_i)$ as $n\to \infty$. 

To achieve this, we use a strengthened version of the coupling between the melons $WB^n$ and the Airy line ensemble provided by Theorem \ref{thm: melon scaling Airy}. In particular, by another application of the Skorokhod representation theorem, we obtain the coupling on page \pageref{Skorokhod coupling}. In particular, from item $2.$ therein, we have the convergence of rescaled geodesic jump times on the melon to those of semi-infinite geodesics on the Airy line ensemble. 

Now, fix $1\le i\le m$. By the stationarity of geodesics under horizontal shifts, item $(4)$ in Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, there exists some random (possibly $i$-dependent) $y\in (\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap(0, \infty))$ such that $Z_\ell(x, y) > 0$. This means almost surely eventually in $n$, the geodesics in $WB^n$ from $(2x_in^{-\frac{1}{3}}, n) \to (1+2yn^{-\frac{1}{3}}, 1)$ reach level $\ell$ before time $1$. 

By item 4. of the above coupling, we have the geodesics in $WB^n$ from $(2x_in^{-\frac{1}{3}}, n) \to (1, 1)$ and $(2x_in^{-\frac{1}{3}}, n) \to (1+2yn^{-\frac{1}{3}}, 1)$ meet at some point $(2Tn^{-\frac{1}{3}}, 1+2d(T)n^{-\frac{1}{3}})$ almost surely eventually in $n$. By the choice of $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}_{> 0}$ and the monotonicity properties of geodesics, we have the geodesics in $WB^n$ from $(2x_in^{-\frac{1}{3}}, n) \to (1, 1)$ and $(2x_in^{-\frac{1}{3}}, n) \to (1, \ell)$ meet at the same point $(2Tn^{-\frac{1}{3}}, 1+2d(T)n^{-\frac{1}{3}})$ almost surely eventually in $n$. Taking $n\to \infty$ and using the local uniform convergence of the melons the rescaled $A^n$ to the Airy line ensemble completes the proof, noting last passage values are continuous with respect to the environment in this local uniform sense.

Now, by the continuity of $\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, \ell)}$, Theorem \ref{thm: busemann cont}, we obtain the desired joint convergence in law on paths on $\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}_>0$ as the Borel sigma algebra of $\mathcal{C}((0, \infty))$ is generated by the evaluation functionals $\pi_x : \mathcal{C}((0, \infty))\to \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}: f\mapsto f(x)$, $x> 0$. 

To prove the second part, one proceeds in an entirely analogous way, adjusting the definitions of $D_{n, \ell}$, $L_{n, \ell}$, $U_\ell(x_i)$ (using the edge scaling of terms in Theorems \ref{thm: melon scaling Airy} and Theorem 8.3 in \cite{DOV}) to accommodate the new endpoint $z$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm: exponential intercept tails}
There exist universal constants $c,C>0$ such that, for every
$x,r>0$, $y\in\mathbb R$, and $k\ge2$,
\[
    \mathbb P\bigl(Z_k(x,y)>y-r\bigr)
    \le
    \min\left\{
        1,\,
        C\exp\bigl(Cx^3+Cr^3-ck\bigr)
    \right\}.
\]
In particular, for $x,y>0$,
\[
    \mathbb P\bigl(Z_k(x,y)>0\bigr)
    \le C\exp\bigl(Cx^3+Cy^3-ck\bigr).
\]
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Fix $k\ge 2$ and $x > 0, y \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. For the stationary Airy line ensemble, use $t\mapsto \mathcal R(t):=\mathcal A(t)+t^2$. As usual, constants denoted by $c,C$ below are positive and universal and may change from line to line.

To prove the desired concentration bounds for the semi-infinite geodesic jump times $Z_k(x, y)$, we will use the structure present in the prelimiting Brownian melon environments converging to the Airy line ensemble from Theorem \ref{thm: melon scaling Airy}.

First, we recall some notation from the discussion preceding the coupling on page \pageref{Skorokhod coupling}. 

Fix compact $I\subset (0, \infty), k \ge 3$ and $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. By the stationarity of geodesics in the Airy line ensemble, Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, we have $Z_k(u, y) \overset d= Z_k(u, 0) + y$. We can thus without loss of generality set $y=0$. We will henceforth assume that there exist unique semi-infinite geodesics with rational endpoints (or on any fixed set of endpoints we use in the remainder of the argument), guaranteed to occur with probability one by Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}. We will also assume the Airy line ensemble is coupled to a version of the Airy sheet $\mathcal{S}$, see \ref{coupling: Airy sheet}. 

Fix $q\in I$ rational. Then, by Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump} and Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, with $Z_k(q, 0)$ the jump time of the semi-infinite geodesic $\pi[q\to (0, 1)]$ from level $k$ to $k-1$ satisfies
\[
Z_k(q, 0) = \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, 0]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, k)}^{(0, 1)}(q)+\mathcal{A}[(s, k)\to (0,1)]\bigg)\,,
\]
Now, for fixed $r>0$, by the metric composition law and the coupling between $\mathcal{A}, \mathcal{S}$, this gives the almost sure equality,
\[
0 = \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(0, 1)}(q)-\mathcal{A}_k(Z_k(q,0))+\mathcal{A}_k(Z_k(q,0)) + \mathcal{A}[(Z_k(q, 0), k) \to (0, 1)]\,.
\]
Since the Busemann functions above are evaluated at rational endpoints, we can express the above as the almost sure limit
\begin{align*}
    \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(0, 1)}(q) &= \lim_{m\to \infty} \mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, m)\to (Z_k(q, 0), k)]+\mathcal{A}_k(Z_k(q,0))\\
    &-\mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, m)\to (0, 1)]\,.
\end{align*}
On the event $\{Z_k(q, 0) > -r\}$, the crossing lemma, Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing} applied to the endpoints $(-r, k), (Z_k(q, 0), k)$ and $(0, 1), (0, k)$, we have
\[
\mathcal{A}_k(Z_k(q,0)) + \mathcal{A}[(Z_k(q, 0), k) \to (0, 1)]\le \mathcal{A}_k(-r) + \mathcal{A}[(-r, k) \to (0, 1)]\,.
\]
Similarly on $\{Z_k(q, 0) > -r\}$, Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing} applied to the points $(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, k), (-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, m)$ and $(Z_k(q, 0) , k), (0,k)$ and taking $m\to \infty$ gives
\[
\Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(0, 1)}(q)-\mathcal{A}_k(Z_k(q,0))\le \Delta_{(0, k)}^{(0, 1)}(q)-\mathcal{A}_k(0)\,.
\]
Combining the above gives on $\{Z_k(q, 0) > -r\}$,
\[
0\le \Delta_{(0, k)}^{(0, 1)}(q)-\mathcal{A}_k(0) + \mathcal{A}_k(-r) + \mathcal{A}[(-r, k) \to (0, 1)]\,.
\]
By Theorem \ref{thm: Busemann equality in law}, we have
\[
 \Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, k)}(q) = F^q_k -\mathcal{S}(q, 0)-\mathcal{A}_k(0)\,,
 \]
 where
 \[
 F^q_k \overset d = \mathcal{A}[(0, k) \to (q, 1)]\,,\qquad q \in (\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap(0, \infty))\,.
 \]
We thus have the inclusion (see figure \ref{fig: geod length bound} for an illustration)
\[
\{Z_k(q, 0)> -r\} \subseteq \{\mathcal{A}_1(0)\le U^q_k + \mathcal{R}_k(-r) + \mathcal{R}[(-r, k) \to (0, 1)]\}\,,
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \mathcal{R}(\cdot) := \mathcal{A}(\cdot) + (\cdot)^2\,, \quad U^q_k &\overset d = \mathcal{A}[(0, k) \to (x, 1)]+x^2\,.
\end{aligned}
\]
Now, for fixed $x> 0$, taking some rational sequence $q> 0$ approximating $x$, using the continuity of the Airy sheet and the almost sure continuity of the Busemann functions $\Delta^{(0, 1)}_{(0, k)}$ at $x$ (in fact, it is almost surely locally constant in a neighbourhood of $x$), we have almost surely the inclusion
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: intercept-inclusion}
    \{Z_k(x, 0)>-r\}
    \subseteq
    \{\chi\le
        U^x_k+V_k+\mathcal R_k(-r)\}\,,
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal R_k(-r)\overset d=\mathcal A_k(0)$ by stationarity and we set $V_k:=\mathcal R[(-r,k)\to(0,1)]$, $\chi:=\mathcal{S}(x,0)+x^2$.
By the proof of Lemma \ref{lemma: finite-depth-tail} (\eqref{eq: moment gen func airy lpp} specifically) and Cauchy--Schwarz we have for any $\lambda > 0$,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb E \mathrm{e}^{\lambda(U^x_k+V_k)}
    &\le
    \left(
        \mathbb E \mathrm{e}^{2\lambda U^x_k}
        \mathbb E \mathrm{e}^{2\lambda V_k}
    \right)^{1/2}\le
    \exp\left[
        C\lambda\sqrt{k}(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{r})
        +C\lambda^2 (x+r)
    \right].
\end{align*}
An application of Markov's inequality to the random variable $\mathrm{e}^{\lambda(U^x_k+V_k)}$, with $\lambda > 0$ chosen appropriately gives
\[
    \mathbb P(U^x_k+V_k\ge u)
    \le
    \exp\left[
        -\frac{
            c\bigl(u-C\sqrt{k}(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{r})\bigr)_+^2
        }{x+r}
    \right]\,,\qquad u > 0\,.
\]

Fix $a>0$ sufficiently small (as required by Lemma \ref{lemma: airy counting 2}) and set $h=ak^{2/3}$. If $\mathcal R_k(-r)\le-4h$ and $\chi\ge-h$, then
\eqref{eq: intercept-inclusion} forces
$U^x_k+V_k\ge3h$. Hence, by a union bound we obtain the estimate
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb P(Z_k(x,0)>-r)
    &\le
    \mathbb P(\mathcal R_k(-r)>-4h)
    +\mathbb P(\chi<-h)+\mathbb P(U^x_k+V_k\ge3h)\\
    &\le
    \mathbb P(\mathcal A_k(0)>-4h)
    +\mathbb P(\chi<-h)+\mathbb P(U^x_k+V_k\ge3h)\,.
\end{align*}
The Airy counting estimate, Lemma \ref{lemma: airy counting 2} and the GUE Tracy--Widom lower
tail of $\chi$ give
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: explicit-intermediate-tail}
\begin{split}
    \mathbb P(Z_k(x,0)>-r)
    \le{}&
    Ce^{-ck\log(k+1)}\\
    &+
    C\exp\left[
        -\frac{
            c\bigl(ak^{2/3}
               -C\sqrt{k}(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{r})\bigr)_+^2
        }{x+r}
    \right],
\end{split}
\end{equation}
where the Tracy--Widom term $Ce^{-ck^2}$ has been absorbed
into the first term. Since $\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{r}\le\sqrt{2(x+r)}$, there is a universal $C_0$ such that, whenever $k\ge C_0(1+(x+r)^3)$ the positive part in \eqref{eq: explicit-intermediate-tail}
is at least a fixed positive multiple of $k^{2/3}$.
Consequently,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: four-thirds-intercept}
    \mathbb P(Z_k(x,0)>-r)
    \le
    Ce^{-ck\log(k+1)}
    +Ce^{-ck^{4/3}/(x+r)},
    \qquad
    k\ge C_0(1+(x+r)^3)\,,
\end{equation}
Under the same restriction on $k$, namely $k\ge C_0(1+(x+r)^3)$ we have $k^\frac{4}{3}/(x+r)\ge c_0 k$ for some constant $c_0$. Thus \eqref{eq: four-thirds-intercept} implies
\[
    \mathbb P(Z_k(x,0)>-r)\le Ce^{-ck}
    \qquad\text{for }k\ge C_0(1+(x+r)^3).
\]
For $k<C_0(1+(x+r)^3)$, the trivial bound by $1$ implies,
after enlarging the universal constants,
\[
    \mathbb P(Z_k(x,0)>-r)
    \le C\exp(Cs^3-ck).
\]
Combining the two regimes gives, for every $k\ge2$,
\[
    \mathbb P(Z_k(x,0)>-r)
    \le C\exp\bigl(C(x+r)^3-ck\bigr).
\]
Finally, the algebraic inequality $(x+r)^3\le4(x^3+r^3)$ and the endpoint translation identity yields
\[
    \mathbb P\bigl(Z_k(x,y)>y-r\bigr)
    \le C\exp(Cx^3+Cr^3-ck).
\]
Taking $r=y>0$ gives the claimed bound for
$\mathbb P(Z_k(x,y)>0)$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm: coal depth}
Fix $x>0$ and $\ell\ge2$. There exist constants $c_x,C_x>0$ such that,
for every integer $n\ge\max\{3,\ell\}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:main}
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(N_\ell(x)>n)
 \le \min\left\{1,
 C_x(1+\ell+\log n)^{2/3}n^{-1/56}
 \exp\bigl(C_x(\log n)^{5/6}\bigr)\right\}.
\end{equation}
In particular, for fixed $x,\ell$ and every $\eta>0$,
\[
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(N_\ell(x)>n)\le C_{x,\ell,\eta}n^{-1/56+\eta}\,.
\]
For $\ell=1$ the paths coincide and one can take $N_1(x)=1$.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop: diff dir separation}
Fix a compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$. For $x\in I$, $y\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$
and $0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_I$, set
\[
 K_\varepsilon(x,y):=
 \inf\left\{k\ge2:Z_k(x-\varepsilon,y)<Z_k(x,y)\right\}.
\]
There exist $c_I,C_I>0$ such that, for all $k\ge3$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Ktail}
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(K_\varepsilon(x,y)>k)
 \le \min\left\{1,
 C_Ik^2\exp\left(-c_I\varepsilon^{4/3}k^{1/21}\right)
 +C_Ik\exp\left(-c_Ik^{1/63}\right)\right\}.
\end{equation}
In particular, for every $M>0$ there exist $B_{I,M},C_{I,M}>0$ such that $ \varepsilon\ge B_{I,M}k^{-1/28}(\log k)^{3/4}$ gives the bounds
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Kpoly}
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(K_\varepsilon(x,y)>k)\le C_{I,M}k^{-M}.
\end{equation}
The constants do not depend on $y$.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lemma: jump parab}
Fix a compact $I\subset(0,\infty)$. For some $c_I,C_I,d_I>0$,
all $u\in I$, $y\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, $k\ge3$ and $0<d<d_I$ satisfy
\begin{align}\label{eq:jump}
 &\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|Z_k(u,y)-y+\sqrt{\frac{k}{2u}}\right|
                 >d\sqrt{k}\right)\notag\\
 &\hspace{15mm}\le
 \min\left\{1,
 C_Ik^2e^{-c_Id^{4/3}k^{1/21}}
 +C_Ike^{-c_Ik^{1/63}}\right\}.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Fix compact $I\subset (0, \infty), k \ge 3$ and $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. By the stationarity of geodesics in the Airy line ensemble, Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, we have $Z_k(u, y) \overset d= Z_k(u, 0) + y$. We can thus without loss of generality set $y=0$. We will henceforth assume that there exist unique semi-infinite geodesics with rational endpoints (or on any fixed set of endpoints we use in the remainder of the argument), guaranteed to occur with probability one by Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}. We will also assume the Airy line ensemble is coupled to a version of the Airy sheet $\mathcal{S}$, see \ref{coupling: Airy sheet}. 

Fix $q, q'\in I$ rational Then, by Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump} and Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, with $Z_k(q, 0)$ the jump time of the semi-infinite geodesic $\pi[q\to (0, 1)]$ from level $k$ to $k-1$ satisfies
\[
Z_k(q, 0) = \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, 0]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q')+\mathcal{A}[(s, k)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\,,
\]
and
\[
Z_k(q', 0) = \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, 0]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q')+\mathcal{A}[(s, k)\to (y,1)]\bigg)\,.
\]
Now, for fixed $r>0$, by the metric composition law and the coupling between $\mathcal{A}, \mathcal{S}$, this gives the almost sure inequality,
\[
\mathcal{S}(q', 0)-\mathcal{S}(q', -r) - \mathcal{S}(q, 0)+\mathcal{S}(q, -r)\ge \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q') - \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q)\,.
\]
Since the Busemann functions above are evaluated at rational endpoints, we can express the above as the almost sure limit
\begin{align*}
    \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q') - \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q) &= \lim_{m\to \infty} \mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q'}, m)\to (Z_k(q, 0), k)]\\
    &-\mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, m)\to (Z_k(q, 0), k)]\\
    &-\mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q'}, m)\to (-r, 1)]+\mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, m)\to (-r, 1)]\,.
\end{align*}
On the event $\{Z_k(q, 0) > -r\}$, we have by the crossing lemma for geodesics, Lemma \ref{lemma: crossing} the lower bound
\begin{align*}
    \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q') - \Delta_{(Z_k(q, 0), k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q) &\ge \lim_{m\to \infty} \mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q'}, m)\to (-r, k)]\\
    &-\mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, m)\to (-r, k)]\\
    &-\mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q'}, m)\to (-r, 1)]+\mathcal{A}[(-\sqrt{m}/\sqrt{2q}, m)\to (-r, 1)]\\
    &=  \Delta_{(-r, k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q') - \Delta_{(-r, k)}^{(-r, 1)}(q)\,.
\end{align*}
By Theorem \ref{thm: Busemann equality in law}, we have
\[
 \Delta^{(-r, 1)}_{(-r, k)}(q')-\Delta^{(-r, 1)}_{(-r, k)}(q)+\mathcal{S}(q', -r)-\mathcal{S}(q, -r)+2z(u-v) \overset d = \mathcal{A}[(0, k) \to (q', 1)] - \mathcal{A}[(0, k) \to (q, 1)]\,.
\]
We thus have the inclusion (for a geometric proof, see Figure \ref{fig: geod inc parab loc})
\[
\{Z_k(q, 0)> -r\} \subseteq \{H^{q, q'} \ge 2r(q-q') + U^{q, q'}_k\}\,,
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
    H^{q, q'} &:= \mathcal{S}(q', 0) - \mathcal{S}(q, 0)\\
    U^{q, q'}_k & \overset d = \mathcal{A}[(0, k) \to (q', 1)] - \mathcal{A}[(0, k) \to (q, 1)] \,.
\end{aligned}
\]
Arguing in an entirely analogous way, we obtain for $0< q'< q$, on the event $\{Z_k(q, 0)< -r\}$ (flipping $q, q'$ in the upper tail event inclusion),
\[
\{Z_k(q, 0) < -r\} \subseteq \{H^{q', q} \ge 2r(q'-q) + U^{q', q}_k\}\,.
\]
Now, for fixed $u, v\in I$, taking rational $q, q'$ approximating $u, v$ respectively and using the continuity of the Airy sheet and the almost sure continuity of the Buseman functions $\Delta^{(-r, 1)}_{(-r, k)}$ at $u, v$ (in fact, it is locally constant in a neighbourhood of $\{u, v\}$), we have almost surely,
\[
\begin{aligned}
    &\{Z_k(u,0)>-r\}\subseteq \{ H^{u, v}  \ge  2r(u-v) + U^{u, v}_k\}\,,\quad 0< u < v\\
    & \{Z_k(u,0)<-r\}\subseteq \{ H^{v, u} \ge 2r(v-u) + U^{u, v}_k\}\,,\quad 0< v < u
\end{aligned}
\]

Fix $d> 0$ and $u \in I$. By a union bound, we can thus estimate 
\begin{align*}
    &\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|Z_k(u,0)+\sqrt{\frac{k}{2u}}\right|
                 >d\sqrt{k}\right)\le \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\left(Z_k(u, 0) < -\sqrt{\frac{k}{2u}}+d\sqrt{k}\right) + \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\left(Z_k(u, 0) > -\sqrt{\frac{k}{2u}}-d\sqrt{k}\right)\,.
\end{align*}
To estimate the first term, taking in the above couplings $r = \sqrt{k}/\sqrt{2u}-d\sqrt{k}$ and $v = u + \theta d$, for some $\theta>0$ sufficiently small to be chosen later, we estimate,
\begin{align*}
    &\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\left(Z_k(u, 0) < -\sqrt{\frac{k}{2u}}+d\sqrt{k}\right)\le \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigg( H^{u, v} -U^{u, v}_k\ge -2d(\sqrt{k}/\sqrt{2u}-d\sqrt{k})\theta\bigg)\\
    &\le \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigg( |H^{u, v}+v^2-u^2| + |U^{u, v}_k+v^2-u^2-2\sqrt{2k}(\sqrt{v}-\sqrt{u})|\ge D\bigg)\,,
\end{align*}
where for $u\in I$, $\theta$ sufficiently small (possibly depending on $I$), possibly depending on $I$,
\[
D = 2\sqrt{k}\left(\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{v}-\sqrt{u}) - d(1/\sqrt{2u}-d)\theta\right) =  2\sqrt{k}\left(\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{u+\theta d}-\sqrt{u}) - \theta d/\sqrt{2u}+ d^2\theta\right)\,.
\]
for some $I$ dependent constant $c_I> 0$.
By Taylor's theorem, we have $\sqrt{u+\theta d}- \sqrt{u}$ $= \theta d /(2\sqrt{u}) + O(d^2\theta^2)$ for all $\theta, d > 0$ sufficiently small. This gives the lower bound
\[
D = 2\sqrt{k}\left(d^2\theta + O(d^2\theta^2)\right) \ge c_I d^2\sqrt{k}\,,
\]
again for $\theta > 0$ sufficiently small.

A union bound, standard GUE Tracy-Widom bounds for the stationary Airy$_2$ process, see Lemma 9.5 in \cite{DOV} give 
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(|H^{u, v}+v^2-u^2|\ge c_I d^2\sqrt{k})\le Ce^{-c(d^2\sqrt{k})^{3/2}}\,.
\]
There exists some $d_I$ so that for all $0< d< d_I$, Lemma~\ref{lemma: shape} applied to the `last passage' term $U^{u, v}_k$, with
\[
 1\le s:=c_Id^{4/3}k^{1/21}\le k^{1/14},\qquad
 k^{3/7}s^{3/2}=c_Id^2\sqrt{k}\,,
\]
gives for all $k$ sufficiently large
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(|U^{u, v}_k+v^2-u^2-2\sqrt{2k}(\sqrt{v}-\sqrt{u})|\ge c_Id^2\sqrt{k})\le C_Ik^2e^{-c_Id^{4/3}k^{1/21}}
 +C_Ike^{-c_Ik^{1/63}}\,.
\]
By enlarging $C_I$, we can trivially extend the above bounds to all $k\ge 3$.

For the lower tail of $Z_k(u, 0)$, use the above coupling with $H^{v, u}$ and $U^{u, v}_k$, taking $r=-\sqrt{k}/\sqrt{2u}-d\sqrt{k}$ and $v=u-\theta d$ and proceed in an entirely analogous way, concluding the proof.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
By monotonicity of geodesics, $Z_k(x-\varepsilon,y)\le Z_k(x,y)$.
Moreover, we have the uniform lower bound on some neighbourhood $U_I$ of $I$ on the positive reals separated away from zero
neighbourhood of $I$,
\[
 \sqrt{\frac{k}{2(x-\varepsilon)}}-
 \sqrt{\frac{k}{2x}}= \sqrt{\frac{k}{2x(x-\varepsilon)}}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{x-\varepsilon}}\ge c_I\varepsilon\sqrt{k}\,,\qquad x\in I, x-\varepsilon \in U_I\,.
\]
Now, if both jump times $Z_k(x-\varepsilon,y), Z_k(x,y)$ lie within $c_I\varepsilon\sqrt{k}/3$ of $-\sqrt{k}/\sqrt{2(x-\varepsilon)}$ and $-\sqrt{k}/\sqrt{2x}$ respectively, then they must be positively separated.
Thus on event $\{K_\varepsilon>k\}$, at least one jump time must have separation at least $c_I\varepsilon\sqrt{k}/3$ from its `parabolic' counterpart. Lemma~\ref{lemma: jump parab} with $d=c_I\varepsilon/3$ gives the estimate in \eqref{eq:Ktail}. 

Fix $M>0$. Observe that for positive $B>0$,
\[
 \left(B k^{-1/28}(\log k)^{3/4}\right)^{4/3}k^{1/21}
   =B^{4/3}\log k.
\]
Choosing $B$ large enough gives the first term in \eqref{eq:Ktail}
is at most $Ck^{-M}$; the second has the same bound after adjusting
$C$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lemma:geometry}
Fix $x>0$, $0<\varepsilon<x$, $Y>0$ and $n\ge\ell$.
Then, outside a null event depending on these fixed parameters, with
\[
E_\varepsilon(0, Y): = \{\mathsf{ZigZag}(\pi[x-\varepsilon\to(0,1)])\text{ and }\mathsf{ZigZag}(\pi[x+\varepsilon\to(Y,1)]) \text{ touch}\}\,,
\]
the following inclusion holds,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:inclusion}
 \{N_\ell(x)>n\}\subseteq
 E_\varepsilon(0,Y)^c
 \cup\{Z_\ell(x+\varepsilon,Y)\le0\}
 \cup\{K_\varepsilon(x,0)>n\}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Suppose the three events on the right do not occur. Write
\[
 \alpha=\pi[x-\varepsilon\to(0,1)],\quad
 \beta=\pi[x\to(0,1)],\quad
 \gamma=\pi[x\to(0,\ell)],\quad
 \delta=\pi[x+\varepsilon\to(Y,1)]\,.
\]
Since $Z_\ell(x+\varepsilon,Y)>0$, $\delta(0)\ge \ell+1$. Geodesic monotonicity gives the pointwise ordering
\[
 \alpha\le\beta\le\gamma\le\delta\qquad\text{on }(-\infty,0].
\]
Moreover, there exists some point $(z, j)$ for $z < 0$, $j\ge \ell+1$ at which the zig-zags of $\alpha$ and $\delta$ touch (see Figure \ref{fig: geod coal depth}). By monotonicity this means all of the zig-zags of $\beta, \gamma$ also meet at $(z, j)$. By Lemma \ref{lemma: geod tree}, $j\le K_\varepsilon(x, 0) \le n$ since otherwise this would produce two distinct semi-infinite geodesics with asymptotic direction $x$ and right endpoint $(0, 1)$, violating the uniqueness of semi-infinite geodesics in the Airy line ensemble, Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}. Now, since by Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, the geodesics $\pi[x\to (0, \ell)]$ and $\pi[x\to (0, 1)]$ eventually coalesce, the same geodesic uniqueness argument gives that they must have already coalesced by level $j \le n$, giving $N_\ell(x)\le n$, concluding the proof.
\begin{figure}[ht]
    \centering
    \includegraphics[width=0.6\linewidth]{Images/geodesic_coal_depth.png}
    \caption{Illustration of a typical realisation of geodesics $\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \delta$ on the event $E_\varepsilon(0, Y)$ where $\alpha$ and $\delta$ touch and the path $\delta$ descends below level $\ell + 1$ on the vertical axis.}
    \label{fig: geod coal depth}
\end{figure}
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Arguing as in the proof of Theorem \ref{prop: non-disj-close-endpts-inf-geo}, we have for any $x> 0, y > 0, \ell \ge 2$,
\[
\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(Z_\ell(x,0)\le-y\bigr) \le C\cdot \exp (\ell - ca^{\frac{3}{2}})\,,
\]
for constants $c, C> 0$. This gives the uniform bounds,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:shield}
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(Z_\ell(u,Y)\le0)
 =\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(Z_\ell(u,0)\le-Y)
 \le C_x\exp\bigl(\ell-c_xY^{3/2}\bigr)\,,\qquad u\in[x/2,3x/2]\,.
\end{equation}

Combining Lemmas~\ref{lemma: close-not-dj} and \ref{lemma:geometry} with
Proposition~\ref{prop: diff dir separation} and a union bound give the estimates
\begin{align}\label{eq: coal bound 1}
 \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(N_\ell(x)>n)
 &\le C_x(1+Y)\varepsilon^{1/2}
             \mathrm{e}^{C_x|\log\varepsilon|^{5/6}}
       +C_xe^{\ell-c_xY^{3/2}}\notag\\
 &\quad+C_xn^2e^{-c_x\varepsilon^{4/3}n^{1/21}}
       +C_xn \mathrm{e}^{-c_xn^{1/63}}\,.
\end{align}
Fix $M=3$. For all sufficiently large $n$, choose
\[
 \varepsilon_n=B_xn^{-1/28}(\log n)^{3/4},\qquad
 Y_n=D_x(1+\ell+\log n)^{2/3},
\]
where $B_x,D_x$ are sufficiently large. Then the last three terms
of \eqref{eq: coal bound 1} are at most $C_xn^{-3}$. The first is at most
\[
 C_x(1+\ell+\log n)^{2/3}n^{-1/56}(\log n)^{3/8}
           \mathrm{e}^{C_x(\log n)^{5/6}} =  C_x(1+\ell+\log n)^{2/3}n^{-1/56}
           \mathrm{e}^{C_x(\log n)^{5/6}}
\]
after enlarging $C_x$. After increasing $C_x$ to cover the finite range where
$\varepsilon_n$ is not sufficiently small, this proves
\eqref{eq:main}. The second expression follows from the fact
$\exp(C(\log n)^{5/6})=n^{o(1)}$.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{corollary: geod two end points disjoint}
 For any  $\ell \ge 1$ and $y_1< y_2$, almost surely
    \[
    \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}\bigg(\bigg\{x>0:  \pi^L[x\to (y_1, 1)]\text{ and } \pi^R[x\to (y_2, \ell)] \text{ are disjoint} \bigg\}\bigg) = \frac{1}{2}.
    \]
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm: airy sheet monotone haus dim}
    The Hausdorff dimension of the topological support of the random measure $\nu$ defined above satisfies almost surely
    \[
    \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathrm{supp}(\nu)) = \frac{1}{2}.
    \]
    Moreover, $\mathrm{supp}(\nu)$ is a closed nowhere dense subset of $\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$.
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
By translation invariance of the Airy sheet $\mathcal{S}(\cdot+ t, \cdot + t) \stackrel{d}{=}\mathcal{S}(\cdot+ t, \cdot + t)$ for any fixed $t\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, \eqref{eq: Airy sheet symmetry} we can without loss of generality take
 \[
    \nu((0, x]\times (0, y]) := \mathcal{S}(x+1, y+1) - \mathcal{S}(x+1,1) - \mathcal{S}(1, y+1) + \mathcal{S}(1, 1)\,.
\]

   \underline{Upper bound}: By Theorem 1.1 of \cite{Hammond_2019}, arguing as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lemma: close-not-dj} using the construction of semi-infinite geodesics with rational endpoints as limits of last passage paths on the prelimiting Brownian melon, that is the coupling on page \pageref{Skorokhod coupling} we obtain for all $\varepsilon>0$, $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq: close-not-dj-near-endpts}
	\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \bigg(\pi[x-\varepsilon \to (y-\varepsilon, 1)] \;\;\; \mathrm{and} \;\;\; \pi[x+\varepsilon \to (y+\varepsilon, 1)] \text{ are disjoint} \bigg) \le C\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)}\,,
	\end{equation}
    for some universal constant $C> 0$.

    By the coupling in \ref{coupling: Airy sheet} between the Airy sheet and the Airy line ensemble, we can express for all $(x, y) \in (0, \infty)\times (0, \infty)$,
    \[
    \nu((0, x]\times (0, y]) = \Delta_{(y+1, 1)}^{(1, 1)}(x+1) - \Delta_{(y, 1)}^{(1, 1)}(1)\,.
    \]
    Notice a quick computation shows for any rectangle $(a, b] \times (a', b']\subset (0, \infty)\times(0, \infty)$, 
     \[
    \nu((a, b]\times (a', b']) = \Delta_{(b'+1, 1)}^{(a'+1, 1)}(b+1) - \Delta_{(b'+1, 1)}^{(a'+1, 1)}(a+1)\,.
    \]
    
    Now, for any $a < b$ finite, consider the rectangle $[a, b]\times [a, b]\subset\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$. For any $\varepsilon > 0$, starting from $a$, partition $[a, b]\times [a, b]$ into a family of intervals of diameter $2\varepsilon$, possibly overshooting $(b, b)$ in the last step. Let $N_\varepsilon\le \lceil(b-a)/(2\varepsilon)\rceil^2$ be the random number of intervals $(I^\varepsilon_i)_i$ above for which $\nu(I^\varepsilon_i)>0$. Arguing as in Proposition \ref{prop: local variation geod disj} we have the equivalence
    \[
    \nu((a, b]\times (a', b'])> 0 \text{ if and only if } \pi[a , a'] \;\;\; \mathrm{and} \;\;\; \pi[b , b'] \text{ are disjoint}\,.
    \]
    Thus, we have by \eqref{eq: close-not-dj-near-endpts} for all $i\le N_\varepsilon$
    \[
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(\nu(I^\varepsilon_i)> 0)\le C\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)}\,.
    \]
    Using linearity and taking expectations, we have using Markov's inequality the estimate
    \[
    \mathbb{E}[N_\varepsilon] \le \bigg\lceil\frac{b-a}{2\varepsilon}\bigg\rceil^2\cdot\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \le C_{a, b}\cdot\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)}
    \]
    for some constant $C_{a, b} > 0$.

    We can thus cover the support of $\nu$ in $[a, b]\times [a, b]$, $\mathrm{supp}(\nu, [a, b]\times [a, b])$ by $N_\varepsilon$ many intervals $(I^\varepsilon_i)_i$ of diameter $2\varepsilon$ such that for any fixed $\delta > 0$,
    \[
    \sum_i \operatorname{diam}(I^\varepsilon_i)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}\le C_\delta  \cdot N_\varepsilon\cdot \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}\,.
    \]
    for some constant $C_\delta > 0$. Taking expectations, we have
    \[
     \mathbb{E}[\sum_i \operatorname{diam}(U^i_\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}]\le C_{a, b, \delta}\cdot \varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)} \cdot \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta} \le C_{a, b, \delta} \cdot \varepsilon^{\delta+ o_\varepsilon(1)}\,, 
    \]
    which converges to zero as $\varepsilon \to 0$. Thus, almost surely, the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathrm{supp}(\nu, [a, b]\times [a, b])$ is at most $1/2 +\delta$. Since $\delta$, $a< b$ were arbitrary, we obtain that the Hausdorff dimension of  $\mathrm{supp}(\nu, (0, \infty) \times (0, \infty))$ is at most $1/2$. To extend the upper bound to $\mathrm{supp}(\nu, \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2)$, use the stationarity of the Airy sheet under uniform shifts in both coordinates, \eqref{eq: Airy sheet symmetry}.
    
\underline{Lower bound}: The lower bound follows from the pathwise regularity of the Airy sheet, in particular that it is H\"{o}lder $1/2^-$ continuous on $\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$ and that the differences $\mathcal{S}(x+1, y+1) - \mathcal{S}(x+1,1) - \mathcal{S}(1, y+1) + \mathcal{S}(1, 1)$ are asymptotically non-degenerate. In fact, by the global shape bounds of the Airy sheet, \eqref{eq: airy shape bnds}, almost surely as $x, y \to \infty$, $\nu((0, x]\times(0, y]) \to \infty$.

Suppose for a contradiction the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathrm{supp}(\nu, \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2)$ is less than $1/2$. Then, we claim on any interval $[a, b]$ $\nu((0, x]\times(0, y])$ must be constant; in particular, it must vanish. Indeed, we estimate for all $\varepsilon > 0$ by the Holder continuity of $\mathcal{S}$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq: mass distribution busemann-two-dim}
         0 < \nu((a, b] \times (a, b]) \le \sum_{i}\nu(I^\varepsilon_i)\le C_{a, b}\cdot\sum_{i}\mathrm{diam}(I^\varepsilon_i)^{\frac{1}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1)}\,,
    \end{equation}
    for some possibly random $C_{a, b}>0$ where $(I^\varepsilon_i)$ cover $(a, b]\times (a, b]$ and have diameter $c\varepsilon$ for some universal $c> 0$. Since we assumed $\mathrm{supp}(\nu, [a, b]\times [a, b])$ has Hausdorff dimension less than $1/2$, say $\delta < 1/2$ we could have chosen the $(I^\varepsilon_i)_i$ so that
    \[
    \lim_{\varepsilon \to 0} \sum_{i}\mathrm{diam}(I^\varepsilon_i)^{\delta + (1/2-\delta)/2} = 0\,.
    \]
    This however, is a contradiction to \eqref{eq: mass distribution busemann-two-dim} since $\frac{1}{2}+o_\varepsilon(1) > \delta + (1/2-\delta)/2$ eventually as $\varepsilon \to 0$. 
    
    Since $a< b$ was arbitrary, we see that either the Hausdorff dimension of the support of $\nu$ on $(0, \infty)\times (0, \infty)$ is $1/2$, or $\nu((0, \infty) \times (0, \infty))=0$, a contradiction to its asymptotic non-degeneracy as discussed above. This concludes the proof of the lower bound.

    Since the Lebesgue measure of $\operatorname{supp}(\nu)$ necessarily vanishes by the above computation of the Hausdorff dimension, $\operatorname{supp}(\nu)$ must have empty interior, and is so nowhere dense.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor: geod disj same endpt}
    Almost surely, the set of directions
    \[
    \mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}}: =  \{(x, y)\in (0, \infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}: \pi^L[x\to (y, 1)] \text{ and } \pi^R[x\to (y, 1)] \text{ are disjoint strictly before }y\}\subseteq (0, \infty) \times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\,.
    \]
    Moreover, its closure, $\overline{\mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}}}$ is a closed, nowhere dense set of Hausdorff dimension equal to $\frac{1}{2}$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
To compute the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}}$, it suffices to observe that almost surely, 
\begin{align*}
    \mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}} = \mathrm{supp}(\nu, (0,\infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}})
\end{align*}
with $\nu$ as in Theorem \ref{thm: airy sheet monotone haus dim}.

\underline{$\subseteq$}: Indeed, by the monotonicity of left most/rightmost geodesics with respect to their endpoints and asymptotic directions, we have that if $(x, y)\in \mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}}$, then for any rational $0<u< x< v$ and $w< y< z$, we have the unique (item $(2)$ in Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}) geodesics $\pi[u\to (w, 1)]$ and $\pi[v\to (z, 1)]$ are disjoint before $y$ (any possibly common vertical interval is taken care of by taking $w< y$). Then arguing exactly as in Proposition \ref{prop: local variation geod disj}, we obtain that $x\in \mathrm{supp}(\nu, (0, \infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}})$. This gives the upper bound of $1/2$ by Theorem \ref{thm: airy sheet monotone haus dim}.

\underline{$\supseteq$}: Now if $x\in  \mathrm{supp}(\nu, (0, \infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}})$ then for any rational $0<u< x< v$ and $w< y< z$ we have the unique (item $(2)$ in Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}) $\pi[u\to (w, 1)]$ and $\pi[v\to (z, 1)]$ are disjoint.

Suppose $(x, y) \notin \mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}}$. Then by geodesic uniqueness in the Airy line ensemble, see the remark after Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp} and the approximation Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx} (see Figure \ref{fig: geod approx}), it cannot be the case that the point of intersection $(z, \ell)$ of the zigzags (recall \eqref{eq: zig-zag}) of $\pi^L[x\to (y, 1)]$ and $\pi^R[x\to (y, 1)]$ occurs after a horizontal segment of both paths. Since if that were the case, there would exist $0<u< x< v$ and $w< y< z$ such that the unique geodesics (item $(2)$ in Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}) $\pi[u\to (w, 1)]$ and $\pi[v\to (z, 1)]$ are not disjoint; in particular, they would share $(z, \ell)$. Arguing as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop: local variation geod disj} would thus contradict $x\in \mathrm{supp}(\nu, (0, \infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}})$. This means the geodesics must be disjoint before $y$, which concludes the proof of this part.

To prove $ \mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}}$ is nowhere dense, observe that by the approximation lemma, Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx}, for all rational pairs $(q, r) \in (0, \infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ the uniqueness of geodesics with such endpoints (item $(2)$ in Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}) guarantees that there exists a an open set $(q,r)\ni U_{q, r} \not \in \mathrm{supp}(\nu, (0,\infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}})$. Closedness follows immediately by the equality previously established, which means $ \mathsf{Disj}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.1ex, scale=1]
    \draw[thick] (-1ex, -0.2ex)--(-1ex, 0.8ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \draw[thick] (0, -0.2ex)--(0,0.8ex) -- (0,1.8ex);
    \fill (-1ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-1ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,-0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0,1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture}
{}}$ is the topological support of a random measure.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{thm: fixed dir coal}
    Fix $x>0$. Almost surely, the set
    \[
        \mathsf{NonUnique}^x_{\uparrow}
        :=
        \left\{y\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}:
        \begin{array}{l}
            \text{there are at least two semi-infinite geodesics}\\
            \text{with direction $x$ ending at $(y,\ell)$ for some $\ell\ge1$}
        \end{array}
        \right\}
    \]
    is at most countably infinite.
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
For $n,\ell\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$, let
    \[
        \mathcal N^x_{n,\ell}
        :=
        \left\{y\in[-n,n]:
        \begin{array}{l}
            \text{there are at least two semi-infinite geodesics}\\
            \text{with direction $x$ ending at $(y,\ell)$}
        \end{array}
        \right\}.
    \]
    Observe Since
    \[
        \mathsf{NonUnique}^x_{\uparrow}
        =
        \bigcup_{n,\ell\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}}\mathcal N^x_{n,\ell}\,,
    \]
    it therefore suffices to prove that $\mathcal N^x_{n,\ell}$ is
    almost surely countable for every fixed $n,\ell\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$.

    By the coalescence of geodesics with fixed asymptotic direction,
    item $(3)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, and
    the ordering of the extremal geodesics, all semi-infinite
    geodesics with direction $x$ ending at $(y,\ell)$,
    $y\in[-n,n]$, eventually coalesce. Indeed, it suffices to
    consider the leftmost geodesic ending at $(-n,\ell)$ and the
    rightmost geodesic ending at $(n,\ell)$.
    We may therefore choose a point $(z,m)$ on this common tail
    with $z<-n$ rational and $m\ge\ell+1$.
    Restricting the geodesics to the portion after $(z,m)$ gives
    \[
        \mathcal N^x_{n,\ell}
        \subseteq
        \left\{y\in[-n,n]:
        \begin{array}{l}
            \text{there are at least two geodesics}\\
            \text{from $(z,m)$ to $(y,\ell)$}
        \end{array}
        \right\}.
    \]

    Since each finite collection of Airy lines is locally
    absolutely continuous with respect to independent rate two
    Brownian motions see Section \ref{sec: preliminaries}, it suffices to prove that, for every
    $n,\ell,m\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$ with $m\ge\ell+1$ and every rational $z<-n$,
    the set
    \[
        \mathsf{NonUnique}^{z,n,m,\ell}_{\uparrow}
        :=
        \left\{y\in[-n,n]:
        \begin{array}{l}
            \text{there are at least two Brownian last passage}\\
            \text{geodesics from $(z,m)$ to $(y,\ell)$}
        \end{array}
        \right\}
    \]
    is almost surely countable.

    Let $B$ be an environment of independent rate two Brownian
    motions. By geodesic uniqueness for deterministic endpoints (see the remark after Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp}),
    we may work on the probability-one event on which, for all
    $m\ge k\ge1$ and all rational $z<q$, the geodesic from
    $(z,m)$ to $(q,k)$ is unique.
    We will proceed by induction on the relative depth
    $m-\ell\ge1$.

    \underline{Base case, $m=\ell+1$}:
    Write
    \[
        W(s):=B_{\ell+1}(s)-B_\ell(s),\qquad
        M(t):=\max_{z\le s\le t}W(s),\qquad
        \widetilde W(t):=M(t)-W(t).
    \]
    By independence, $W$ is a rate four Brownian motion.
    By the metric composition law,
    Lemma~\ref{lemma: Metric Composition}, the possible jump
    times from level $\ell+1$ to level $\ell$ are precisely in the set
    \[
        A(y):=\operatorname*{argmax}_{z\le s\le y}W(s),
    \]
    whose minimum and maximum are
    $\inf A(y):= Z^L_m((z,m),(y,\ell))$ and
    $\sup A(y):= Z^R_m((z,m),(y,\ell))$, respectively.
    Incidentally, by L\'evy's theorem,
    see \cite[Theorem~2.34]{morters_peres_2010},
    $\widetilde W(z+\cdot)$ has the law of the absolute value
    of a rate four Brownian motion started at zero.

    Observe that the set of maximisers $A(y)$ cannot contain two points $a<b<y$ as this would violate geodesic
    uniqueness from $(z,m)$ to $(q,\ell)$.
    Hence, if $A(y)$ is not a singleton, then $A(y)=\{a,y\}$ for some $a < y$. In this case,
    \[
        \widetilde W(a)=\widetilde W(y)=0,
        \qquad
        \widetilde W(t)>0\quad\text{for }a<t<y,
    \]
    so $(a,y)$ is an excursion of $\widetilde W$ away from zero.
    Conversely, if $(a,b)$ is such an excursion with $b<\infty$,
    then $A(b)=\{a,b\}$.
    Thus $\mathsf{NonUnique}^{z,n,m,\ell}_{\uparrow}$
    consists precisely of the finite right endpoints in
    $[-n,n]$ of excursions of $\widetilde W$ away from zero.
    There are at most countably many such excursions, since
    $\{t>z:\widetilde W(t)>0\}$ is open.
    This concludes the proof of the base case.

    \underline{Induction step, $m\ge\ell+2$}:
    For $k\in\{\ell,\ell+1\}$ and $s\ge z$, write
    \[
        G_k(s):=B[(z,m)\to(s,k)],
        \qquad
        H(s):=G_{\ell+1}(s)-B_\ell(s).
    \]
    By the metric composition law, Lemma \ref{lemma: Metric Composition}
    \[
        G_\ell(y)
        =
        \max_{z\le s\le y}
        G_{\ell+1}(s)+B_\ell(y)-B_\ell(s)
        =
        B_\ell(y)+\max_{z\le s\le y}H(s).
    \]
    Thus the possible jump times from level $\ell+1$ to
    level $\ell$ are precisely in the set
    \[
        B(y):= \operatorname*{argmax}_{z\le s\le y}H(s).
    \]
    Observe that with the gap process
    \[
        \widetilde H(y)
        :=
        \max_{z\le s\le y}H(s)-H(y)
        =
        G_\ell(y)-G_{\ell+1}(y)\,,
    \]
    the excursion argument from the base case applies to $H, \tilde{H}$. Observe for any $y\in [-n, n]$, the set $B(y)$ cannot contain two $ z \le a  < b< y$, as this would violate uniqueness. Hence, whenever the jump time at $y$ is not unique, the maximizers in $B(y)$ are exactly $\{a,y\}$, where $(a,y)$ is an
    excursion of $\widetilde H$ away from zero.
    In particular, the set
    \[
        \mathcal E
        :=
        \left\{y\in[-n,n]:
        Z^L_{\ell+1}((z,m),(y,\ell))
        <
        Z^R_{\ell+1}((z,m),(y,\ell))
        \right\}
    \]
    is countable, by continuity.

    Now fix $y\in[-n,n]\setminus\mathcal E$ and let
    \[
        s
        :=
        Z^L_{\ell+1}((z,m),(y,\ell))
        =
        Z^R_{\ell+1}((z,m),(y,\ell)).
    \]
    Suppose, $s<y$, then all geodesics from $(z,m)$ to $(y,\ell)$
    share the horizontal segment from $(s,\ell)$ to $(y,\ell)$.
    Choose a rational $q\in(s,y)$.
    Their restrictions to $(q,\ell)$ are geodesics from
    $(z,m)$ to $(q,\ell)$, and so coincide.
