    Since their remaining portions are the same horizontal
    segment, the original geodesics are identical as well.

    If $s=y$, any two distinct geodesics
    from $(z,m)$ to $(y,\ell)$ restrict to two distinct
    geodesics from $(z,m)$ to $(y,\ell+1)$.
    We therefore have
    \[
        \mathsf{NonUnique}^{z,n,m,\ell}_{\uparrow}
        \setminus\mathcal E
        \subseteq
        \mathsf{NonUnique}^{z,n,m,\ell+1}_{\uparrow}.
    \]
    The set on the right is countable by the induction
    hypothesis, and $\mathcal E$ is countable.
    This concludes the inductive step and the proof.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\label{thm: geod no coal h dim}
    Set
    \[
    \mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}
    :=
    \bigcup_{\substack{t<t'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\\m\le m'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}}}
    \left\{x>0:
    \begin{aligned}
        &\pi^L[x\to(t,m)] \text{ and } \pi^R[x\to(t',m')]\\
        &\text{do not coalesce}
    \end{aligned}
    \right\}.
    \]
    Then, there exists an event $\Omega_0$ of full probability
    on which $\mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}$ has Hausdorff dimension
    equal to $\frac12$. However,
    \[
    \mathsf{NoCoalTouch}_{ 
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
                               
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
    
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}
    :=
    \bigcup_{\substack{t<t'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\\m\le m'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}}}
    \left\{x>0:
    \begin{aligned}
        &\pi^L[x\to(t,m)] \text{ and } \pi^R[x\to(t',m')]\\
        &\text{do not coalesce and are not disjoint}
    \end{aligned}
    \right\}
    \]
    is at most countable on $\Omega_0$.
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
We start by computing the Hausdorff dimension of
$\mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}$.

\underline{Lower bound}: To establish the lower bound in the
Hausdorff dimension of $\mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}$, observe that
by Proposition \ref{prop: local variation geod disj}, almost
surely, for all $\ell \ge 1$,
\[
    \left\{x>0:
    \begin{aligned}
        &\pi^L[x\to(0,1)] \text{ and } \pi^R[x\to(0,\ell)]\\
        &\text{are disjoint}
    \end{aligned}
    \right\}
    =
    \mathsf{LV}\bigl((0,\infty),
    \Delta^{(0,1)}_{(0,\ell)}\bigr).
\]

This means $\mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}$ contains the set of
local variation of $\Delta^{(y_2,1)}_{(y_2,1)}$, $y_1<y_2$,
which by Corollary \ref{corollary: geod two end points disjoint}
has Hausdorff dimension equal to $1/2$.

\underline{Upper bound}: Observe that by geodesic monotonicity
and uniqueness (item $(2)$ in
Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}), we have
\[
    \mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}
    \subseteq
    \bigcup_{n\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}}
    \left\{x>0:
    \begin{aligned}
        &\pi^L[x\to(-n,1)] \text{ and } \pi^R[x\to(n,1)]\\
        &\text{do not coalesce}
    \end{aligned}
    \right\}.
\]
By Proposition \ref{prop: local variation geod disj},
$\mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}\setminus\mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}$ is thus contained in the union
of the sets of local variation of
$\Delta^{(-n,1)}_{(n,1)}$, $n\ge1$, which by
Corollary \ref{corollary: geod two end points disjoint}
have Hausdorff dimension equal to $1/2$. 

We now prove that the set of geodesic non-disjointness and no coalescence $\mathsf{NoCoalTouch}_{ 
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
                               
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
    
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}$
is at most countable. This would then yield the upper bound on the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathsf{NoCoal}_{
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    
    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (1.2ex, -0.2ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (1.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}$.

By the approximation lemma,
Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx} and geodesic monotonicity, we have
\[
    \mathsf{NoCoalTouch}_{ 
  \begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=0.3ex, scale=1]
                               
    \coordinate (origin) at (1.2ex, 1.8ex);

    \draw[thick] (0.2ex, 1.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (-0.8ex, 0.8ex)--(-0.8ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (0.2ex, 1.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (-0.8ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \draw[thick] (origin) -- (1.2ex, 0.8ex) -- (0.2ex, 0.8ex)-- (0.2ex, -0.2ex);

    \fill (1.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, 0.8ex) circle (0.2ex);
    \fill (0.2ex, -0.2ex) circle (0.2ex);

    \fill (origin) circle (0.2ex);
    
  \end{tikzpicture} 
}
    =
    \bigcup_{\substack{t<t'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\\m\le m'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}}}
    \left\{x>0:
    \begin{aligned}
        &\pi^L[x\to(t,m)] \text{ and } \pi^R[x\to(t',m')]\\
        &\text{do not coalesce and are not disjoint}
    \end{aligned}
    \right\}.
\]
We thus consider fixed $t<t'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}$, $m\le m'\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$ in
the remainder of the proof and show that, almost surely,
\[
    \left\{x>0:
    \begin{aligned}
        &\pi^L[x\to(t,m)] \text{ and } \pi^R[x\to(t',m')]\\
        &\text{do not coalesce and are not disjoint}
    \end{aligned}
    \right\}
\]
is at most countable.

We start with the following observation. If
$\pi^L[x\to(t,m)]$ and $\pi^R[x\to(t',m')]$ do not
coalesce, then by geodesic uniqueness in the Airy line
ensemble for geodesics with rational endpoints (see the
remark after Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp}),
eventually the geodesics must become disjoint. Now, if they
were not entirely disjoint, then for every asymptotic direction $x$
in the set above, we can associate an open subset
$\mathcal{U}_x\subset(0,\infty)\times\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ that is the space
between the geodesics $\pi^L[x\to(t,m)]$ and
$\pi^R[x\to(t',m')]$ after their last point of contact
(see Figure \ref{fig: geod coal not disj} for an illustration).

Moreover, we claim that for all $x\neq y$,
$\mathcal{U}_x\cap\mathcal{U}_y=\emptyset$. This would
give an injection of the set above into $\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap (0, \infty)$,
concluding the proof of countability.

\begin{figure}[ht]
    \centering
    \includegraphics[width=0.65\linewidth]{Images/geod_countable_different_endpts_2.eps}
    \caption{Illustration of non-coalescing geodesics
        which are not disjoint and the space between
        them given by open sets $\mathcal{U}_x$.}
    \label{fig: geod coal not disj}
\end{figure}

Indeed, let $x<y$. Then, observe that $\mathcal{U}_x$ is
bounded to the right by $\sigma$, which is a path given
by the concatenation of
$\pi^L[x\to(t,m)]|_{[s,t]}$ with
$\pi^R[x\to(t',m')]|_{(-\infty,s]}$, where $(s,\ell)$
is the last point of contact of the zig-zags of $\pi^L[x\to(t,m)]$ and
$\pi^R[x\to(t',m')]$
(see Figure \ref{fig: geod coal not disj}). Now, consider
the path $\pi^L[y\to(t,m)]$, which bounds $\mathcal{U}_y$
from the left. We claim that we have the ordering
$\sigma(z)\le\pi^L[y\to(t,m)](z)$ for all $z\le t$.

Now, by geodesic monotonicity, we have
\[
    \sigma|_{[s,t]}
    =
    \pi^L[x\to(t,m)]|_{[s,t]}
    \le
    \pi^L[y\to(t,m)]|_{[s,t]}
\]
pointwise. In particular,
$\sigma(s)=\pi^L[x\to(t,m)](s)
\le\pi^L[y\to(t,m)](s)$.
Moreover, by geodesic monotonicity
(by geodesic uniqueness, see the remark after Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp},
see also Figure \ref{fig: geod approx}), we have the
ordering (see Figure \ref{fig: geod coal not disj})
\begin{align*}
    \sigma|_{(-\infty,s]}
    &=
    \pi^R[x\to(t',m')]|_{(-\infty,s]}\\
    &\le
    \pi^R[x\to(s,\ell)]|_{(-\infty,s]}\\
    &\le
    \pi^L[y\to(s,\ell)]|_{(-\infty,s]}\\
    &\le
    \pi^L[y\to(t,m)]|_{(-\infty,s]}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop: multiple geod fixed endpt}
    Fix $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. There exists an event $\Omega_0$ of full probability such that on $\Omega_0$, the set of directions
    \[
    \mathsf{NonUnique}^y_{\downarrow}: = \{x> 0: \pi^L[x\to (y, 1)] \neq  \pi^R[x\to (y, 1)]\}
    \]
    is a dense countably infinite subset of $(0, \infty)$.
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
The same holds replacing $(y, 1)$ with $(y, \ell)$, $\ell \ge 1$.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
As usual work with event on which all geodesics with rational directions are unique and for all rational asymptotic directions any two semi-infinite geodesics sharing the former eventually coalesce. 
    
       We now show $\mathsf{NonUnique}^y_{\downarrow}$ is dense in $(0, \infty)$, hence at least countably infinite.

       Suppose for a contradiction that it is not dense. Let $0< u< v$ be such that for all $w\in [u, v]$ there is a unique $w$-directed semi-infinite geodesic, that is $\pi^L[w\to (y, 1)] = \pi^R[w\to (y, 1)]$. By monotonicity of geodesics, for $\ell \ge 2$ the geodesic jump times $Z^\square_\ell (w, y)$, $w\in [u, v]$ are monotone and by the second part of the approximation lemma, piecewise constant. By the supposed geodesic uniqueness on $[u, v]$ this means the jump times $Z^\square\ell(\cdot, y)$ coincide and so are continuous. This means that they must all coincide. Since $\ell$ was arbitrary, this means in fact that all geodesics $\pi[w\to (y, 1)]$ is a contradiction to the asymptotic parabolic trajectories of semi-infinite geodesics, in Definition \ref{def: semi-inf geo}.

       The countability of $\mathsf{NonUnique}^y_{\downarrow}$ follows by a similar argument using the monotonicity of the geodesic jump times above.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- theorem-11: body_md -->
\label{thm: geodesic tree}
    There exists an event of full probability $\Omega_0$ such that on $\Omega_0$, the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow$ is $1$ simultaneously for all $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. 
    
    Moreover, for any fixed $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, almost surely $\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow$ contains no loops and there exists an at most countably infinite family $\Delta_n$, $n\ge 1$ of pairwise disjoint `wedge-like' open sets such that $\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cup_n \Delta_n =  (-\infty, y]\times[1, \infty)$.

    Finally, under the scaling
    $\Psi_k: (x, y) \mapsto (1/k x, 1/k^2 y)$ $k\ge 1$, we have for any fixed $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ almost surely that
    \[
    \bigcup_{k\ge 1}\Psi_k\circ \mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow
    \]
    is dense in $(-\infty, 0] \times [1, \infty)$. In particular, for any bounded and open subset $U\subset (-\infty, 0]\times [1, \infty)$ (with respect to the subspace topology), the Hausdorff distance of the closures 
    \[
    d_{\mathrm{Hausdorff}}(\overline{\cup_{1\le k \le M}\Psi_k\circ \mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cap U}, \overline{U}) \to 0
    \]
    as $M\to \infty$.
<!-- end theorem-11 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Since horizontal segments of geodesics lie in $\cup_n(-\infty, y]\times \{n\}$, and so have Hausdorff dimension $1$, it suffices to compute the Hausdorff dimension of the vertical portions of the zig-zags of geodesics.
     
    Almost surely, for all $x\ge 0$, $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ by Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx}, the left-most and right most semi-infinite geodesics $\pi^L[x\to (y, \ell)], \pi^R[x\to (y, \ell)]$ from $x$ to $(y, \ell)$ can be expressed as the pointwise limits
    \[
     \pi^L[x\to (y, \ell)] = \sup_{\substack{n\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\,,\\\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\ni z_n \nearrow x}}\pi^\square[z_n, (y, \ell)]\text{ and } \pi^R[x\to (y, \ell)] = \inf_{\substack{n\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\,,\\\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\ni w_n \searrow x}}\pi^{\square}[w_n, (y, \ell)].
    \]
    Moreover, almost surely, for any $t\le y$, the restrictions $\pi^\square[z_n, (y, 1)]|_{[t, y]}$ and $\pi^\square[w_n, (y, 1)]|_{[t, y]}$ agree with $\pi^L[x\to (y, 1)]|_{[t, y]}$ and $\pi^R[x\to (y, 1)]|_{[t, y]}$ for all $n$ sufficiently large (depending on $t$). 

    This means almost surely, for all $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ and any $x>0, \ell\ge 2$, there exists an open interval $\mathcal{U}_x$ in $[0, \infty)$ (in the subspace topology) such that the geodesic jump times in $\mathcal{T}^y_{\downarrow}\cap \mathcal{U}_x\times [\ell, \ell-1]$ coincide with those of $\pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)]$. This means, using a compactness argument and the fact that $\ell\ge 2$ is arbitrary that any fixed compact subset of the plane away from $\{y\}\times[1, \infty)$ contains finitely many vertical geodesic segments of $\mathcal{T}^y_{\downarrow}$.
    
     We are now in a position to compute the full Hausdorff dimension of $\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow$. To do this we have by monotonicity of the Hausdorff dimension
    \[
    \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow) = \sup_{\ell\ge 2}\mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times [\ell, \ell-1])\,.
    \]
    We can drop the endpoints $\ell, \ell-1$ as they do not contribute to the Hausdorff dimension (they only account for horizontal segments of geodesics which take discrete values). 
    \[
    \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow) = \sup_{\ell\ge 2}\mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times (\ell, \ell-1))\,.
    \]
    We can now express $\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times (\ell, \ell-1) = A_\ell\times (\ell, \ell-1)$, where $A_\ell$ is the set of jump times $Z^\square_\ell(x, y)$, $x\ge 0$. Note by the above we have computed 
    $\mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(A_\ell)=1$ for all $\ell \ge 2$ (we showed in fact $A_\ell$, $\ell\ge 2$ are all countable). 
    
    Thus, by standard arguments, see \cite{Hatano1971Notes} we can estimate
    \begin{align*}
    1=&\mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(A_\ell)+ \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}((\ell, \ell-1)) \le \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times (\ell, \ell-1))\\
    \le &\min\{1+ \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(A_\ell), 1+ \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}((\ell, \ell-1))\} =1
    \end{align*}
    concluding that the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathcal{T}^y_\uparrow$ is $1$.

    For the reminder, we fix $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. 
    
    Observe that by geodesic uniqueness in the Airy line ensemble for finite horizon geodesics with fixed endpoints, almost surely $T^y_\uparrow$ cannot contain any loops (see Figure \ref{fig: geod approx}).
    
    Now, consider the set of directions $x> 0$ for which geodesics $\pi^L[x\to (y, 1)]$ and $\pi^R[x\to (y, 1)]$ are not identical. On $\Omega_0$, there are at most countably many such asymptotic directions $x> 0$ (by Proposition \ref{prop: multiple geod fixed endpt}). Now, let $(x_n)_n$ be such a sequence (if it exists). Let $(\Delta_n)_n$ denote the interiors between the zig-zags of $\pi^L[x_n\to (y, 1)]$ and $\pi^R[x_n\to (y, 1)]$ after their last point of contact. Moreover, observe that taking $x\to 0$, we have by monotonicity of jump times that $\pi^R[x\to (y, 1)]$ converges to a parabolic path with asymptotic direction $0$, namely $\pi^R[0
    \to (y, 1)]$. If this path is not identically equal to $\pi^L[0\to (y, 1)]\equiv 1$, then add $\Delta_0$ defined as above. 

    As $x\to \infty$, for all $\ell \ge 2$, we have that the geodesic jump times $Z_\ell(x, y)\to 0$. For a proof, see Proposition \ref{prop: non-degen asymptotic}. Moreover, by monotonicity of jump times and the above, it follows that any point $(x, z)\in (-\infty, y]\times [1, \infty)$ lies either on the zig-zag of some parabolic path or is in some $\Delta_n$, concluding the proof of this part.

    For the final part, fix any bounded and open set $U$ of $(-\infty, 0]\times [0, \infty)$ (with respect to to the subspace topology). By the inclusion for all $M\ge 1$, $\cup_{1\le k \le M}\Psi_k\circ \mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cap U\subset U$ it suffices to show for any $\varepsilon> 0$, the $\varepsilon$-neighbourhood of $\Psi_k\circ \mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\cap U$ contains $U$. By compactness, for all $\varepsilon'>0$, the closure of $U$, $\overline{U}$ can be covered by the $\varepsilon'$-neighbourhoods of $(t_i, 2x_it_i^2)$ for finitely many $x_i, t_i$.

    Observe that the asymptotic parabolic shape of geodesics $\lim_{t\to -\infty} \pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)](t)/t^2 = 2x$ simultaneously for all $(x, y)\in [0, \infty) \times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ means that for any fixed $x\ge 0, y\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, the rescaled graph of the geodesic $\pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)]$, $\Psi_k\circ \pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)](\cdot) = 1/k^2\cdot \pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)](k \cdot(\cdot))$ converges pointwise to $2xt^2$ as $k\to \infty$. Thus, taking $k\to \infty$ sufficiently large so that for all $x_i, t_i$, $\Psi_k\circ \pi^\square[x_i\to (y, 1)](t_i)$ are within $\varepsilon'/2$ of $(t_i, 2x_it_i^2)$, the $3/2\varepsilon'$-neighbourhood of $\Psi_k\circ \mathcal{T}^y_{\uparrow}$ contains $U$ for all $k$ sufficiently large. Since $\varepsilon'$ was arbitrary, this concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor: geod jump piecewise constant}
    As a consequence, for fixed $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, we obtain that for all $\ell \ge 2$ the jump times $(0, \infty)\ni x\mapsto Z^{\square}_\ell(x, y)$ are piecewise constant. Since they cannot be globally constant as they converge to zero as $x\to \infty$, this means that there must exist asymptotic directions $x> 0$ such that geodesics $\pi^{\square}[x\to (y, 1)]$ are not identical. Moreover, since we can take $Z^{\square}_2(x, y)$ to be a.s. positive for all $x> 0$ on $\Omega_0$, we have the set of asymptotic directions for which geodesic non-uniqueness occurs is \emph{countably infinite}.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{def: ergodicity}
Suppose $L$ is a horizontal shift-invariant process on the probability space $ (\Omega, C , \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} ) $. We say $ A\in C$ is \textbf{shift-invariant} if $ \theta_s\circ A:=\{\theta_s \circ\omega\,| \omega \in A  \}  =A $ for all $ s \in\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}} $. Then $ L $ is \textbf{ergodic} if for all shift-invariant $ A $, $ \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}[A] =0 $ or 1.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- theorem-12: body_md -->
(\cite[Theorem 1.6]{corwin2014ergodicityairylineensemble})\label{thm: erg airy line ensemble}
    The stationary Airy line ensemble is ergodic with respect to horizontal shifts.
<!-- end theorem-12 -->
<!-- theorem-13: body_md -->
\label{thm: ergodic geodesic tree}
    For $n\ge 1$, consider the map $T_n :(x, y) \mapsto (x+n, y)$. Then, for any open subset $U$ with respect to the Fell topology of closed subsets of $\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$ containing an element of the topological support of $\mathcal{T}^0_\downarrow$, almost surely the shifted trees $T_{n}\circ\mathcal{T}^{-n}_\downarrow$, are infinitely often in $U$. 

    Moreover, for any $f: \mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2) \to \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ bounded measurable almost surely,
    \[
    \lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n f(\theta_{-n}\circ\mathcal{T}^{0}_{\downarrow}) = \int_{\mathcal{K}}f \ensuremath{\operatorname{d}\!}\nu\,,
    \]
    where $\nu$ denotes the common law on $\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2)$ (on the associated Borel sigma algebra) of $\theta_{-n}\circ\mathcal{T}^{0}_{\downarrow}$, $n\ge 1$.
<!-- end theorem-13 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
Observe that by the approximation lemma, Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx} all geodesics in the tree $\mathcal{T}^0_\downarrow$ can be obtained as pointwise limits of geodesics with rational asymptotic directions $\pi[q \to (0, 1)]$ for $q\ge 0$. By item (4) in Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, the laws of $\theta_{-n}\circ \mathcal{T}^0_\downarrow$ are therefore identical for all $n\ge 1$. In particular, again by item (4) of Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}, we have almost surely for all $n\ge 1$, $\theta_{-n}\circ \mathcal{T}^0_\downarrow = \theta_{-n}\circ \mathcal{T}^0_\downarrow$ as elements of $\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2)$. Also observe the trees $\theta_{-n}\circ \mathcal{T}^0_\downarrow$ depend on the sigma algebra
    \[
    \sigma\bigg(\{\mathcal{A}_i(x): i\ge 1\,, x\le -n \}\bigg)\,.
    \]
    Thus, for $U$ as above, we have $\nu(U)> 0$. This means for all $n\ge 1$, 
    \[
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(\theta_{-n}\circ\mathcal{T}^{0}_{\downarrow}\in U) = \nu(U)> 0
    \]
    and so
    \[
    \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(\theta_{-n}\circ\mathcal{T}^{0}_{\downarrow}\in U\text{ infinitely often}) \ge \limsup_n \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}(\theta_{-n}\circ\mathcal{T}^{0}_{\downarrow}\in U) = \nu(U)> 0\,.
    \]
    Thus, the event $\theta_{-n}\circ\mathcal{T}^{0}_{\downarrow}\in U\text{ infinitely often}$ is shift invariant (see Definition \ref{def: ergodicity}). Using Theorem \ref{thm: erg airy line ensemble} we see that it must occur almost surely, being positive.
    
    The second part follows from an application of Birkhoff's ergodic theorem, treating for $f$ bounded measurable, $f(\mathcal{T}^{0}_{\downarrow})$ as measurable functionals of the Airy line ensemble (which can be done by the above discussion).
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- theorem-14: body_md -->
\label{thm: geodesic gasket}
    There exists an event of full probability $\Omega_0$ such that on $\Omega_0$, the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow$ is $\frac{3}{2}$ simultaneously for all $x > 0$ . 
    
    Moreover, leftmost/rightmost collections of geodesics $\mathcal{T}^{x, \square}_{\uparrow}$, have a tree-like structure (they contain no loops). Thus, $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow$ can be constructed as a union of two trees.
<!-- end theorem-14 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
Since the Hausdorff dimension of the horizontal segments of geodesics in $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow$ is $1$, we restrict our attention to computing the Hausdorff dimension of `horizontal fibres' $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow$.

We will work on the almost sure event of geodesic existence from item $(1)$ in lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties} and the remark after Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp}.

    \underline{Upper bound}: 

    Fix $x> 0$. We will estimate the Hausdorff dimension of vertical segments of geodesics in $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow \cap [1, \infty) \times [1, 2]$, that is the collection of last jump times of semi-infinite geodesics in with asymptotic direction $x$ and endpoint $y$ at least $1$. Observe that for any such path, that is $\pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)]$, the restriction property of semi-infinite geodesics implies that $\pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)]|_{[0, y]}$ is a leftmost/rightmost geodesic in the Airy line ensemble from $(0, \pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)](0))$ to $(y, 1)$. By the metric composition law, Lemmas \ref{lemma: Metric Composition} and \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp} this means in particular that the last jump times $Z^\square_2(x, y)$ satisfy
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in \operatorname*{argmax}_{0\le s\le y}\mathcal{A}[(0, \pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)](0))\to (s, 2)]+\mathcal{A}_1(y)-\mathcal{A}_1(s)
    \]
    or, equivalently
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in \operatorname*{argmax}_{0\le s\le y}\mathcal{A}[(0, \pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)](0))\to (s, 2)]-\mathcal{A}_1(s)\,.
    \]
    To establish the upper bound on the Hausdorff dimension of $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow \cap [1, \infty) \times [1, 2]$, it thus suffices to show almost surely for al $\ell \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$, the Hausdorff dimension
    \[
    \bigg\{\operatorname*{argmax}_{0\le s\le y}\mathcal{A}[(0, \ell)\to (s, 2)]-\mathcal{A}_1(s): y\ge 1\bigg\}
    \]
    is at most $1/2$. By monotonicity of the Hausdorff dimension this is equivalent to showing the Hausdorff dimension of
    \[
    \bigg\{\operatorname*{argmax}_{0\le s\le y}\mathcal{A}[(0, \ell)\to (s, 2)]-\mathcal{A}_1(s): y\in [n, n+1]\bigg\}
    \]
    is at most $1/2$ almost surely for all $\ell, n\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$.

    Indeed, by the Brownian Gibbs property of the Airy line ensemble (recall from subsection \ref{subsec: Airy line ensemble}), the first $\ell$ levels of the Airy line ensemble are absolutely continuous with respect to $\ell$ independent rate two Brownian motions on $[n, n+1]$. This means it suffices to show for an environment of independent Brownian motions $B$
    \[
    \bigg\{\operatorname*{argmax}_{0\le s\le y}B[(0, \ell)\to (s, 2)]-B_1(s): y\in [n, n+1]\bigg\}
    \]
    is at most $1/2$ almost surely for all $\ell, n\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$. By independence of the lines in the Brownian environment and the distributional equality of $B[(0, \ell)\to (\cdot, 2)]$ to an $\ell-1$-level Dyson Brownian motion \cite{o2002representation}, which is mutually absolutely continuous with respect to rate two Brownian motion on compacts away from the origin (recall from subsection \ref{subsec: melons}), it suffices to show the Hausdorff dimensions of 
    \[
    \bigg\{\operatorname*{argmax}_{0\le s\le y}W(s): y\in [n, n+1]\bigg\}
    \]
    are at most $1/2$ almost surely for all $\ell, n\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$ where $W$ is now a rate four Brownian motion. Since any such time is a record time, it suffices to bound the Hausdorff dimension of record times 
    \[
    \bigg\{s\in [0, n+1]: W(s) = \max_{0\le r\le s}W(r)\bigg\}\,.
    \]
    By L\'evy's theorem for reflected Brownian motion, see \cite[Theorem~ 2.34]{morters_peres_2010}, $\max_{0\le r\le s}W(r)-W(s)$ has the same law on paths on $\mathcal{C}_0 [0, \infty)$ as $|W(\cdot)|$. Hence, since the Hausdorff dimension of the zeros of Brownian motion has Hausdorff dimension at most $1/2$ on any bounded interval, we conclude the about the record times, thereby establishing the desired upper bounds on the Hausdorff dimension. 

    To conclude the proof of the first part, observe one can by similar arguments consider $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow \cap [y, \infty) \times [1, 2]$ almost surely simultaneously for all $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ by repeating the argument replacing the origin with any rational $r< y$. The argument for $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow \cap [y, \infty) \times [\ell, \ell-1]$ proceeds entirely analogously, as one is reduced to considering the last jump times of geodesics ending at level $\ell-1$. 

    \underline{Lower bound}: To establish the lower bound, we need a slight refinement of Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump} for the jump times $Z^\square_2(x, y)$. 

    Proposition \ref{prop: semi-inf geod jump} gives almost surely for any fixed $x> 0, y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in  \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in (-\infty, y]}\bigg(\Delta_{(s, 1)}^{(0, 1)}(x)+\mathcal{A}_1(y)-\mathcal{A}_1(s)\bigg)\,.
    \]
    We will show this to be simultaneous for all $x> 0, y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$.
    
    Lemma 2.24 in \cite{dauvergne2022disjointoptimizersdirectedlandscape} gives almost surely simultaneously for all $x > 0$ and $y< y'\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$ and any parabolic path $\pi:(-\infty, y]\to \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}$ with $\lim_{z\to -\infty}\pi(z)/z^2 = 2x$,
    \[
    \lim_{z\to -\infty}\mathcal{A}[(z, \pi(z))\to (y, 1)]-\mathcal{A}[(z, \pi(z))\to (y', 1)] = \mathcal{S}(x, y)-\mathcal{S}(x,y')\,.
    \]

    Now, taking $\pi$ to be leftmost/rightmost geodesics $\pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)]$, we have by the metric composition law for all $z\le Z^\square_2(x, y)$
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in  \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in [z, y]}\bigg(\mathcal{A}[(z, \pi(z))\to (s, 1)]+\mathcal{A}_1(y)-\mathcal{A}_1(s)\bigg)
    \]
    or equivalently, for any fixed $\alpha < Z^\square_2(x, y)$, 
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in  \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in [\alpha, y]}\bigg(\mathcal{A}[(z, \pi(z))\to (s, 1)]-\mathcal{A}[(z, \pi(z))\to (y, 1)]-\mathcal{A}_1(s)\bigg)\,.
    \]
    By the preceding discussion we have the pointwise convergence for $\mathcal{A}[(z, \pi(z))\to (s, 1)]-\mathcal{A}[(z, \pi(z))\to (y, 1)]\to \mathcal{S}(x, s)-\mathcal{S}(x, y)$ as $z\to -\infty$. This means taking $z\to -\infty$,
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in [\alpha, y]}\bigg(\mathcal{S}(x, s)-\mathcal{A}_1(s)\bigg)\,,
    \]
    or 
    \[
    Z^\square_2(x, y) \in \displaystyle\operatorname*{argmax}_{s\in [\alpha, y]}\bigg(\mathcal{S}(x, s)-\mathcal{S}(0, s)\bigg)\,.
    \]
    Since $\alpha<Z^\square_2(x, y)$ was arbitrary we finally have the first jump times $Z^\square_2(x,y)$ are the locations of the maxima of 
    \[
    s\mapsto \mathcal{S}(x, s)-\mathcal{S}(0,s) \text{ on } (-\infty, y].
    \]
    
    By shape estimates for Airy sheet \eqref{eq: airy shape bnds}, have that as $y\to \infty$, the above maximum diverges so arguing as in the proof of the lower bound in Theorem \ref{thm: airy sheet monotone haus dim} (that is by a `mass distribution' argument) we obtain the set of local variation of 
    \[
    s\mapsto \max_{0\infty< r\le s}\mathcal{S}(x, r)-\mathcal{S}(0,r) = \mathcal{S}(x, s)-\mathcal{S}(0,s)
    \]
    has Hausdorff dimension $1/2$. Since on this set, we have that the argmax must be unique, we deduce the set $ \{Z^\square_2(x, y): y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\}$ must contain this set of local variation, which has Hausdorff dimension at least $1/2$.

    We have thus established that the Hausdorff dimension of first jump times of of geodesics in $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow$ is $1/2$ simultaneously for all $x>0$. 
    
    We now compute the full Hausdorff dimension of $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow$. To do this we have
    \[
    \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow) = \sup_{\ell\ge 2}\mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times [\ell, \ell-1])\,.
    \]
    We can drop the endpoints $\ell, \ell-1$ as they do not contribute to the Hausdorff dimension (they only account for horizontal segments of geodesics which take discrete values). 
    \[
    \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow) = \sup_{\ell\ge 2}\mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times (\ell, \ell-1))\,.
    \]
    We can now express $\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times (\ell, \ell-1) = A_\ell\times (\ell, \ell-1)$, where $A_\ell$ is the set of jump times $Z^\square_\ell(x, y)$, $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. Note by the above we have computed 
    \[
    \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(A_\ell)\le \frac{1}{2}\text{ and } \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(A_2) = \frac{1}{2}\,.
    \]
    Thus, by standard arguments, see \cite{Hatano1971Notes} we can estimate
    \begin{align*}
    &\mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(A_\ell)+ \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}((\ell, \ell-1))(= \frac{3}{2} \text{ when }\ell = 2)\le \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow\cap \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}\times (\ell, \ell-1))\\
    \le &\min\{1+ \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}(A_\ell), 1+ \mathrm{dim}_{\mathscr{H}}((\ell, \ell-1))\} (= \frac{3}{2} \text{ when }\ell = 2)\,.
    \end{align*}
    
    For the last part, this follows from the fact that two geodesics $\pi^\square[x\to (y_1, 1)]$ and $\pi^\square[x\to (y_2, 1)]$ with $y_1< y_2$ $\square\in \{L, R\}$ are either entirely disjoint of coalesce for an interval and then become separate. If there were any `loops', the the uniqueness of leftmost/rightmost geodesics (item $(1)$ in Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties}) would be violated.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- theorem-15: body_md -->
\label{thm: geodesic network haus dim}
    Almost surely, the Hausdorff dimension of the \emph{full semi-infinite geodesic network} \emph{excluding} extremal ` $0$' and `$\infty$'-directed geodesics
    \[
    \mathcal{T}: = \bigcup_{(x,y)\in [0, \infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}}\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow\cup \bigg(\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow\setminus \big(\{y\}\times [1, \infty)\cup (-\infty, y]\times \{1\}\big) \bigg)  \text{ is } \frac{3}{2}\,.
    \]
<!-- end theorem-15 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
The lower bound of $3/2$ is immediate from Theorem \ref{thm: geodesic gasket}. 

    To prove the upper bound, one can proceed in the same way as for the proof of the upper bound in Theorem \ref{thm: geodesic gasket} to estimate the Hausdorff dimensions of 
    \[
    \bigcup_{(x,y)\in [0, \infty)\times \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}}\mathcal{T}^x_\uparrow\cup \mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow \cap [n, n+1]\times [1, \infty)\,, n\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{Z}}\,.
    \]
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2)$ be the family of all closed subsets of $\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$, including the empty set. The topology generated by
\[
 \{F:F\cap U\ne\varnothing\}\quad(U\subseteq\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2\text{ open})\,,
 \qquad
 \{F:F\cap K=\varnothing\}\quad(K\subseteq\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2\text{ compact})\,,
\]
is called the Fell topology.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{prop: fell}
The Fell topology is generated by a complete metric $d_F$. Hence, $(\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2), d_F)$ is a Polish space. Moreover, its Borel sigma-field is generated by the open-set hitting events $\{F:F\cap U\ne\varnothing\}$.
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lemma: jump meas}
For all $j\ge 2$, $q\in (\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap(0, \infty))$ and fixed $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$, the semi-infinite geodesic jump times $Z_j(q,y)$ are random variables.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
Fix $j\ge 2, q\in(\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap(0, \infty))$. Recall from Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties} the existence of the almost surely unique $q$-directed semi-infinite geodesic $\pi[q\to(y,1)]$ with right endpoint $(y, 1)$. Lemma \ref{lemma: semi-inf geod properties} also implies that the semi-infinite jump times $Z_j(q,y)$ are almost sure limits of the finite-horizon jump times from level $j$ to $j-1$ of geodesics from $(-\sqrt{q/(2k)}, k)$ to $(y, 1)$ as $k\to \infty$. It thus suffices to that the jump times of geodesics over the Airy line ensemble from $(a, k)$ to $(b, 1)$ for $a < b$ finite and $k\ge 1$, are random variables. 

Fix $a< b$ finite and $k\ge 1$. Since $(\Omega,\mathscr F,\mathbb P)$ is assumed complete, by the remark after \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp} there exists an event $\Omega_0\in \mathscr{F}$ of full probability such that there exists a unique geodesic from $(a, k)$ to $(b, 1)$. By Lemma \ref{lemma: geod location brown env lpp}, the jump times are the now unique locations of maximisers over last passage values over the Airy line ensemble, which are random variables, concluding the proof.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- theorem-16: body_md -->
\label{thm: meas}
On the complete probability space $(\Omega, \mathscr{F}, \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}})$, the map
\[
 \omega\longmapsto \mathcal{T}^{y}_\downarrow(\omega)
\]
is measurable from $(\Omega,\mathscr F)$ to $(\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2),\mathcal B(d_F))$.
<!-- end theorem-16 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
Fix $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$. We first show the closure of the dense subtree of $\mathcal{T}^y_\downarrow$, $\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow$ is measurable from $(\Omega,\mathscr F)$ to $(\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2),\mathcal B(d_F))$.

Observe that for every open $U\subseteq\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$ and $T\subseteq \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$, the event $T$ hits $U$ is the same as the event $cl_{\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2}(T)$ hits $U$. Hence
\begin{align}\label{eq: hit}
 \{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow\cap U\ne\varnothing\}=\{\mathcal{T}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow\cap U\ne\varnothing\}
 &=\{[1, \infty)\cup (-\infty, y]\times \{1\}\cap U\ne\varnothing\}\nonumber\\
 &\cup\bigcup_{q\in(\ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}\cap(0, \infty))}\{\mathsf{ZigZag}(\pi[q\to (y, 1)])\cap U\ne\varnothing\}\,.
\end{align}
By the measurability of the jump times of the geodesic $s\pi[q\to (y, 1)]$, Lemma \ref{lemma: jump meas}, every event on the right is measurable. Thus by Proposition, \ref{prop: fell}, $\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow$ is measurable from $(\Omega,\mathscr F)$ to $(\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2),\mathcal B(d_F))$.

We now show that $\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow$ and $\mathcal{T}^{y}_\downarrow$ coincide on an event of full probability. Henceforth, we will work on the event of full probability which guarantees the existence and uniqueness of semi-infinite geodesics in the Airy line with rational asymptotic directions and right endpoint $y$. 

Theorem \ref{thm: geodesic tree} gives $\mathcal{T}^{y}_\downarrow$ is a closed subset of $\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2$. This means in particular we have the inclusion $\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow\subseteq \mathcal{T}^{y}_\downarrow$, since $\mathcal{T}^{y}_\downarrow$ contains all geodesic with rational asymptotic directions (as well as the two extremal, that is zero and $\infty$-directed geodesics). To prove the inclusion $\mathcal{T}^{y}_\downarrow\subseteq  \widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow$, observe that by the approximation lemma, Lemma \ref{lemma: geodesic approx}, a point in the closure of $\mathcal{T}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow$ is either in $\{[1, \infty)\cup (-\infty, y]\times \{1\}$, or is a limit of points on the zig-zags of geodesic with rational asymptotic directions. In the latter case, this means the aforementioned points lie up to some fixed depth. By passing to a monotone subsequence of such asymptotic directions, the above lemma implies that the point lies on some $\pi^\square[x\to (y, 1)]$, $x > 0$, concluding the proof of equality thus establishing that on a set of full probability (that is the set of geodesic uniqueness in the Airy line ensemble with rational endpoints also including $y\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}$) we have, $\mathcal{T}^{y}_\downarrow = \widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{y, \ensuremath{\mathbb{Q}}}_\downarrow$ is measurable from $(\Omega,\mathscr F)$ to $(\mathcal{F}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{R}}^2),\mathcal B(d_F))$.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
